We describe this
k
-linear mapping in two steps by defining it on
\mathcal{C}_{k}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}
separately, and then adding them to obtain the desired
k
-linear mapping on
\mathcal{V}^{s\times s}
.

Define the
k
-linear mapping
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}:\mathcal{C}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{s\times s}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k})

\displaystyle=(\varphi\circ\varrho_{1})(Z^{1})\mathcal{G}_{k-1}(\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{2}),\ldots,\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{k}))-\mathcal{G}_{k-1}((\varphi\circ\varrho_{1})(Z^{1})\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{2}),\ldots,\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{k}))

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=2}^{k-1}[\mathcal{G}_{k-1}(\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{1}),\ldots,\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{j-1})(\varphi\circ\varrho_{1})(Z^{j}),\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{j+1}),\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{j+2}),\ldots,\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{k}))

\displaystyle-\mathcal{G}_{k-1}(\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{1}),\ldots,\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{j-1}),(\varphi\circ\varrho_{1})(Z^{j})\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{j+1}),\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{j+2}),\ldots,\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{k}))]

\displaystyle+\mathcal{G}_{k-1}(\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{1}),\ldots,\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{k-1})(\varphi\circ\varrho_{1})(Z^{k}))-\mathcal{G}_{k-1}(\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{1}),\ldots,\varrho_{1}^{\prime}(Z^{k-1}))(\varphi\circ\varrho_{1})(Z^{k}).

It turns out from this definition of
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}
together with the fact that each of
\varphi,\varrho_{1}
and
\varrho_{1}^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{C}(Y)
-bi-module homomorphism that the canonical intertwining conditions hold up to order
k
. Further, from part (i) of Lemma
3.1
it follows that
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}
satisfies Equation (
3.10
). So it remains to estimate the complete norm of
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}
.

Note from the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}
above that

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{k}_{cb}}\leq 2\left\|{\varphi\circ\varrho_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k-1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k-1}_{\text{cb}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=2}^{k-1}2\left\|{\varphi\circ\varrho_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k-1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k-1}_{\text{cb}}+2\left\|{\varphi\circ\varrho_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k-1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k-1}_{\text{cb}}

\displaystyle=2k\left\|{\varphi\circ\varrho_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k-1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k-1}_{\text{cb}}\leq 2^{k}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\varphi\circ\varrho_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k-1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}.

Next, we define the
k
-linear mapping
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}:\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{s\times s}
satisfying the equation

\mathcal{T}_{0}\star\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k})=\mathcal{F}_{k}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k})-\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{T}_{k-j}\star\mathcal{G}_{j}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k}),

for any
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}
, as follows. Fix a basis
\mathcal{B}
of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{1}
and observe that
\mathcal{B}^{\otimes k}
is a basis for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}
. For any
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}\in\mathcal{B}
, we first define
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})
and then extend it to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}
by linearity. So let
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}\in\mathcal{B}
and consider the sequence
\{X_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset\Omega_{(k+1)s}
defined by

X_{n}:=\begin{bmatrix}Y&t_{n}\xi_{1}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&Y&t_{n}\xi_{2}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&0\\
0&0&\cdots&Y&t_{n}\xi_{k}\\
0&0&\cdots&0&Y\end{bmatrix}

where
\{t_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is a sequence of positive real numbers converging to
0
. Then note that
\{X_{n}\}
converges to
Y^{\oplus(k+1)}
and consequently, there exists
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
n\geq N_{0}
,
X_{n}\in{\mathbb{B}}_{nc}(Y,r^{\prime})
. It turns out from the Taylor-Taylor series of
\mathcal{T}
around
Y
for
0\leq j\leq k-1
, that

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}0&\ldots&0&\mathcal{T}_{k-j}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k-j})\star\mathcal{G}_{j}(\xi_{k-j+1},\ldots,\xi_{k})\\
0&\ldots&0&0\\
\vdots&\ldots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&\ldots&0&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t_{n}^{k-j}}\left[\mathcal{T}(X_{n})-\mathcal{T}(Y^{\oplus(k+1)})-\sum_{i=1}^{k-j-1}(X_{n}-Y^{\oplus(k+1)})^{\odot_{i}}\mathcal{T}_{i}\right]\star A^{j}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})

where
A^{j}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})=(\!(A^{j}_{st})\!)_{s,t=0}^{k}
is the
(k+1)\times(k+1)
block matrix with
s\times s
blocks
A^{j}_{st}
defined to be
0
whenever
t-s\neq j
and for
s=1,\ldots,k+1-j
,
A^{j}_{s,s+j}=\mathcal{G}_{j}(\xi_{s},\ldots,\xi_{j+s-1})
. Now for each
\ell=0,1,\ldots,k-1
, we use the Taylor-Taylor series of
\mathcal{F}
around
Y
and Equation (
3.10
) to obtain

\displaystyle K:=\begin{bmatrix}0&\ldots&0&\mathcal{F}_{k}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})-\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{T}_{k-j}\star\mathcal{G}_{j}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})\\
0&\ldots&0&0\\
\vdots&\ldots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&\ldots&0&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t_{n}^{k}}\left[\mathcal{F}(X_{n})-\mathcal{T}(X_{n})\star H^{\oplus(k+1)}-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}t_{n}^{i}\mathcal{T}(X_{n})\star A^{i}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})\right].

Denote

K_{n}=\frac{1}{t_{n}^{k}}\left[\mathcal{F}(X_{n})-\mathcal{T}(X_{n})\star H^{\oplus(k+1)}-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}t_{n}^{i}\mathcal{T}(X_{n})\star A^{i}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})\right]

and observe that
K_{n}\in\mathrm{ran}~\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{T}(X_{n}),\cdot)
. Moreover, it follows from the equation above that
K_{n}
converges to
K
. Therefore,
K\in\mathrm{ran}~\mathfrak{m}\left(\mathcal{T}(Y^{\oplus(k+1)})\right)
implying that there exists
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k})\in\mathcal{Y}^{s\times s}
such that

\mathcal{T}_{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{k}))=K.

Now define

\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k}):=Q(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k})),~Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}

and observe from the condition (b) in the hypothesis together with the equation above that
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}
satisfies Equation (
3.10
). Also, observe from part (ii) in Lemma (
3.1
) that
\mathsf{S}\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k})-\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(\mathsf{S}Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k})
,
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k-1},Z^{k}\mathsf{S})-\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k})\mathsf{S}
and
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{j-1}
,
Z^{j}\mathsf{S}
,
Z^{j+1},\ldots,Z^{k})-\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{j},\mathsf{S}Z^{j+1},Z^{j+2},\ldots,Z^{k})
are in the
\ker\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{T}_{0},\cdot)
for all
Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}
and
\mathsf{S}\in\mathcal{C}(Y)
. Then another application of condition (b) yields that

( 3.11 )

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(\mathsf{S}Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k}),

( 3.12 )

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k-1},Z^{k}\mathsf{S})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k})\mathsf{S},

( 3.13 )

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{j-1},Z^{j}\mathsf{S},Z^{j+1},\ldots,Z^{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{j},\mathsf{S}Z^{j+1},Z^{j+2},\ldots,Z^{k}),

for all
Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}
and
\mathsf{S}\in\mathcal{C}(Y)
.

To estimate the complete norm
\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}
, we first estimate the norm
\left\|{\mathcal{T}_{0}\star\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}
as follows.

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{T}_{0}\star\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{n_{0},n_{1},\ldots,n_{k}}\left\|{(\mathcal{T}_{0}\star\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}})^{(n_{0},n_{1},\ldots,n_{k})}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{k}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{n_{0},n_{1},\ldots,n_{k}}\bigg[\sup_{\left\|{Z_{1}}\right\|_{n_{0},n_{1}}=\cdots=\left\|{Z_{k}}\right\|_{n_{k-1},n_{k}}=1}\left\|{(Z_{1}\odot\cdots\odot Z_{k})\mathcal{T}_{0}\star\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C\sup_{n_{0},n_{1},\ldots,n_{k}}\bigg[\sup_{\left\|{Z_{1}}\right\|_{n_{0},n_{1}}=\cdots=\left\|{Z_{k}}\right\|_{n_{k-1},n_{k}}=1}\left\|{(Z_{1}\odot\cdots\odot Z_{k})\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\alpha\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}.

Here, we use Equation (
3.15
) to obtain the inequality in the third equation and
\alpha=\mbox{min}\{1,C\}
as mentioned earlier in this proof. Consequently, we have that

\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\left\|{\mathcal{T}_{0}\star\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}=\frac{1}{\alpha}\left\|{\mathcal{F}_{k}-\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\mathcal{T}_{k-j}\star\mathcal{G}_{j}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}\\
\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\bigg[\left\|{\mathcal{F}_{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\left\|{\mathcal{T}_{k-j}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k-j}}\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{j}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{j}}\bigg]\leq\frac{M}{\alpha}\bigg[\frac{1}{\tilde{\hat{r}}^{k}}+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{j}M^{j}}{\alpha^{j}\tilde{\hat{r}}^{k}}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}\bigg]\\
\leq\frac{M^{k}}{\alpha^{k}\tilde{\hat{r}}^{k}}\bigg[1+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{j}\alpha^{k-j}}{M^{k-j}}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}\bigg]\leq\frac{M^{k}}{\alpha^{k}\tilde{\hat{r}}^{k}}\bigg[1+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}2^{j}\bigg]\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}\\
\leq\frac{2^{k}M^{k}}{\alpha^{k}\tilde{\hat{r}}^{k}}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}=\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}\hskip 174.89326pt

Thus the desired
k
-linear mapping
\mathcal{G}_{k}:(\mathcal{V}^{s\times s})^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}^{s\times s}
is defined as

\mathcal{G}_{k}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k}):=\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}(\varrho_{k}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k}))+\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}(\varrho^{\prime}_{k}(Z^{1},\ldots,Z^{k})).

Since
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}
satisfies Equations (
3.11
), (
3.12
) and (
3.13
) as well as
\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}
together with
\mathcal{G}_{0},\mathcal{G}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{G}_{k-1}
satisfy the canonical intertwining conditions up to order
k
, it follows that the canonical intertwining conditions hold for
\mathcal{G}_{0},\ldots,\mathcal{G}_{k}
up to order
k
. Finally, we show that
\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{k}_{\text{cb}}}
is bounded above by
r^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}
with the help of the bounds on
\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}\circ\varrho_{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}
and
\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}\circ\varrho^{\prime}_{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}
as demonstrated below.

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}\circ\varrho_{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}+\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}\circ\varrho^{\prime}_{k}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}\left\|{\varrho_{k}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}+\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}\left\|{\varrho^{\prime}_{k}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}(1+\left\|{\varrho^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}})+\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}\left\|{\varrho^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}+\left(\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}+\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{k}}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\text{cb}}^{k}}\right)\left\|{\varrho^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{k}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\varphi\circ\varrho_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k-1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(2^{k}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\varphi\circ\varrho_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\mathcal{G}_{k-1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}+\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}\right)\left\|{\varrho^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{k}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\varphi\circ\varrho_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}\tilde{r}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(2^{k}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}\left\|{\varphi\circ\varrho_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}\tilde{r}^{\prime}+\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\text{max}\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\}\right)\left\|{\varrho^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{k}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(2^{k}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}+\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}\right)\left\|{\varrho^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{k}\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}}\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}(1+2\left\|{\varrho^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|^{k}_{\text{cb}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle[2\left\|{\varrho_{1}^{\prime}}\right\|_{\text{cb}}(1+2\left\|{\varrho^{\prime}_{1}}\right\|_{\text{cb}})]^{k}\tilde{r}^{\prime-k}\text{max}\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle r^{\prime-k}\mathrm{max}\big\{1,\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{Y}}\big\}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

(1) When the dimension of
\mathcal{V}
is finite and
Y\in\Omega_{s}\subset\Omega\subset\mathcal{V}_{nc}
is a semi-simple point,
\mathcal{C}(Y)
-bi-modules turn out to be semi-simple. Consequently, being a
\mathcal{C}(Y)
-bi-module in a natural way,
\mathcal{V}^{s\times s}
is semi-simple. Also, note that the map
\mathfrak{c}:{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}^{s\times s}
– defined by
\mathsf{S}\mapsto[\mathsf{S},Y]
– admits a
\mathcal{C}(Y)
-bi-module right inverse
\varphi:\mathcal{C}_{1}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
. Thus,
\varphi
gives rise to a
\mathcal{C}(Y)
-sub-bi-module
\mathcal{C}_{1}^{\prime}:=\ker\varphi
complementary to
\mathcal{C}_{1}
in
\mathcal{V}^{s\times s}
.

(2) Given Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{V},\mathcal{W},\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{K}
, set
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K})
,
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{H})
and
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{K})
equipped with the natural operator space structure. Let
\mathfrak{m}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}
be the multiplication operator obtained by the composition operator and fix
H\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{H})^{s\times s}
. Then for any uniformly analytic nc functions

\mathcal{T}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K})_{nc}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{F}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{K})_{nc}

satisfying the hypothesis of Theorem
3.4
, there exist a uniformly open nc neighbourhood
U
of
Y
and a uniformly analytic function
\mathcal{G}:U\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{H})_{nc}
such that

\mathcal{G}(Y)=H\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{T}(X)\circ\mathcal{G}(X)=\mathcal{F}(X),\quad\text{for all}~~X\in U.

Observe from CSPS theorem
[
16
, Theorem 1.5.7, pp. 32]
that this is essentially the Theorem
3.4
when
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{K})
for some Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{W}
and
\mathcal{K}
.

In particular, when
\mathcal{W}={\mathbb{C}}
,
\mathcal{Y}
and
\mathcal{Z}
becomes
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{K}
, respectively, equipped with the column operator space structure. In this case, it can be seen as in Lemma
3.6
and Lemma
3.7
in the following subsection that the hypothesis on
\mathfrak{m}
and
\mathcal{T}
in Theorem
3.4
are automatic.

(3) As a special case taking
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{K}={\mathbb{C}}
, we see that
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{H}_{r}
equipped with the row operator space structure and
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
. This particular case will be of interest in Section
5
and the hypothesis on
\mathfrak{m}
and
\mathcal{T}
in Theorem
3.4
for this case are automatically satisfied as we show in the following subsection.

3.2.
Application to the row operator Hilbert space

In this subsection, we briefly recall the notion of the
row operator space structure
on a Hilbert space as well as present an
open mapping type result
(see Proposition
3.7
) in a row operator space, which we then use to obtain a simpler version of Theorem
3.4
in the case of row Hilbert operator spaces.

Recall that a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
gives rise to a canonical isometry between the conjugate Hilbert space and its Banach dual given by

\theta:\overline{\mathcal{H}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{*},\quad\overline{\xi}\mapsto f_{\xi},\quad f_{\xi}(\eta):=\langle\eta,\xi\rangle,

and by Riesz representation theorem we have that
\left\|{f_{\xi}}\right\|=\left\|{\xi}\right\|
. We now define the
Row isometry

\mathsf{Row}:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{**}(=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}^{*},{\mathbb{C}})=\mathcal{B}(\overline{\mathcal{H}},{\mathbb{C}}))
by

\xi\mapsto\mathsf{Row}(\xi)(\overline{\eta}):=\theta(\overline{\xi})(\eta)=\langle\eta,\xi\rangle.

Being an operator space,
\mathcal{B}(\overline{\mathcal{H}},{\mathbb{C}})
induces an operator space structure on
\mathcal{H}
, which is known as the
row operator space
structure.

For any
s\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the
s
-th matrix norm on
\mathcal{H}^{s\times s}
by

\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:=\left\|{\mathsf{Row}_{s}(H)}\right\|,\quad\mathsf{Row}_{s}:\mathcal{H}^{s\times s}\to\mathcal{B}\left(\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{s},{\mathbb{C}}^{s}\right)

is the amplification of
\mathsf{Row}
(cf. Subsection
2.3.2
). Note that
\left\|{H}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}
is the operator norm of
H
viewed as an operator in
\mathcal{B}(\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{s},{\mathbb{C}}^{s})
. Thus this family
\{\|\cdot\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}\}_{s=1}^{\infty}
of norms on
\mathcal{H}
indeed satisfy the conditions (
R1
) and (
R2
) mentioned in Subsection
2.1.2
making
\mathcal{H}
an operator space known as the
row Hilbert operator space
. We denote it by

\mathcal{H}_{r}=\left(\mathcal{H},\left\{\|\cdot\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}\right\}_{s\in\mathbb{N}}\right).

Also, note that the adjoint operator of the row isometry acts as

\mathsf{Row}(\xi)^{*}:{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{H}},\quad\alpha\mapsto\alpha\overline{\xi},

and consequently, its
s
-th amplification acts as

\mathsf{Row}_{s}(H)^{*}:{\mathbb{C}}^{s}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{s},\quad\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{s}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mapsto\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha_{1}&\cdots&\alpha_{s}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\overline{H},~~H\in\mathcal{H}^{s\times s}.

In general, for a given operator space
\mathcal{V}
, the space of matrices
\mathcal{V}^{n\times n}
over
\mathcal{V}
is a Banach space for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Consequently, there is no canonical notion of the “orthogonal projections” onto a closed subspace of
\mathcal{V}^{n\times n}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
. However, when
\mathcal{V}
is a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
endowed with the
row operator space structure
, the Hilbert space structure on
\mathcal{H}
canonically induces a notion of orthogonal projections onto a closed subspace of
\mathcal{H}^{n\times n}
with
n\in\mathbb{N}
as we point out in the following lemma.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathcal{N}\subset\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}
be a closed subspace.

(i)

For any
H\in\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}
, there exists unique
N_{0}\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}
such that

\inf\left\{\left\|{H-N}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}=\left\|{H-N_{0}}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}.

(ii)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
(\!(H_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}\in\mathcal{H}_{r}^{ns\times ns}
with
H_{lk}\in\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}
,

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\left\|{(\!(H_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}-(\!(N_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}}\right\|_{ns,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:(\!(N_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}\in(\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1})^{n\times n}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\|{(\!(H_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}-(\!(N^{0}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}}\right\|_{ns,\mathcal{H}_{r}}

where for each
l,k=1,\ldots,n
,
N^{0}_{lk}
is the unique element in
\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}
such that

\inf\left\{\left\|{H_{lk}-N_{lk}}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:N_{lk}\in\mathcal{N}^{1\times s}\right\}=\left\|{H_{lk}-N^{0}_{lk}}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}.

(iii)

The mapping
Q:\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}
defined by
Q(H):=H-N_{0}
is linear where
N_{0}
is as defined in (i).

Proof.

(i) We begin by pointing out that

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\left\|{H-N}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\sup\left\{\left\|{\mu\overline{H}-\mu\overline{N}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}}:\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s},\left\|{\mu}\right\|\leq 1\right\}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{\inf\left\{\left\|{\mu\overline{H}-\mu\overline{N}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}:\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s},\left\|{\mu}\right\|\leq 1\right\}.

Since
\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}
(isometrically isomorphic to the direct sum
\mathcal{H}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathcal{H}
of
s
copies of
\mathcal{H}
) is a Hilbert space, for each
\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
with
\left\|{\mu}\right\|\leq 1
, there exists unique
n^{0}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{N}
such that

\inf\left\{\left\|{\mu\overline{H}-\mu\overline{N}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}=\left\|{\mu\overline{H}-\bar{n}^{0}_{\mu}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}}.

Denote
n^{0}_{\varepsilon_{j}}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}n^{0}_{j1}&n^{0}_{j2}&\ldots&n^{0}_{js}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathcal{N}
, where
\{\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{s}\}
is the standard ordered basis for
{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
. We now show, for any
\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
, that there exists
(\!(\bar{n}^{0}_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}
satisfying

\bar{n}^{0}_{\mu}=\mu(\!(\bar{n}^{0}_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}.

Note that for any
\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
, the subspace
\mathcal{N}
can be realized as

\overline{\mathcal{N}}=\{\mu\overline{N}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\}

where
\overline{\mathcal{N}}=\{\bar{n}:n\in\mathcal{N}\}
. Indeed, the set in the right hand side is contained in
\overline{\mathcal{N}}
and for any
\bar{n}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\bar{n}_{1}&\ldots&\bar{n}_{s}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}
and non-zero
\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{s})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
with
\mu_{l}\neq 0
for some
l
, the vector
\bar{n}
can be written as
\mu(\!(\bar{n}^{\prime}_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}
where
\bar{n}^{\prime}_{ij}
is
0
if
i\neq l
and
\bar{n}^{\prime}_{lj}=\frac{\bar{n}_{j}}{\mu_{l}}
. Consequently, for any fixed
\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
, we have that

\inf\left\{\left\|{\mu\overline{H}-\mu\overline{N}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}=\inf\left\{\left\|{\mu\overline{H}-\bar{n}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}}:n\in\mathcal{N}\right\}=\left\|{\overline{P}(\mu\overline{H})}\right\|

where
P:\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
is the orthogonal projection and
\overline{P}:\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{s}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{N}}^{\perp}
. Next, we compute this infimum as follows

\left\|{\overline{P}(\mu\overline{H})}\right\|=\left\|{\overline{P}\left(\sum_{l=1}^{s}\mu_{l}\varepsilon_{l}\overline{H}\right)}\right\|=\left\|{\sum_{l=1}^{s}\mu_{l}\overline{P}(\varepsilon_{l}\overline{H})}\right\|=\left\|{\sum_{l=1}^{s}\mu_{l}(\varepsilon_{l}\overline{H}-\bar{n}_{\varepsilon_{l}})}\right\|\\
=\left\|{\sum_{l=1}^{s}\mu_{l}[\varepsilon_{l}\overline{H}-\varepsilon_{l}(\!(\bar{n}^{0}_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}]}\right\|=\left\|{\mu\overline{H}-\mu(\!(\bar{n}^{0}_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}}\right\|.

From the uniqueness of
n_{\mu}
it then follows that
\bar{n}_{\mu}=\mu(\!(\bar{n}^{0}_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}
verifying the claim. Now recalling the identities obtained at the beginning of the proof, we have that

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\left\|{H-N}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{\sup\left\{\left\|{\mu\overline{H}-\mu\overline{N}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}}:\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s},\left\|{\mu}\right\|\leq 1\right\}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sup\!\left\{\left\|{\mu\overline{H}-\mu(\!(\bar{n}^{0}_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}}\right\|:\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s},\left\|{\mu}\right\|\leq 1\right\}=\left\|{H-(\!(n^{0}_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}

completing the proof of (i) with
N_{0}=(\!(n^{0}_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}
.

(ii) Following the same line of argument as in part (i) we have that

\inf\left\{\left\|{(\!(H_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}-(\!(N_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}}\right\|_{ns,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:(\!(N_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}\in(\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1})^{n\times n}\right\}\\
=\sup\left\{\inf\left\{\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\nu_{1}&\cdots&\nu_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right](\!(\overline{H}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}-\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\bar{\eta}_{1}&\cdots&\bar{\eta}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}:\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\eta_{1}&\cdots&\eta_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathcal{N}^{\oplus n}\right\}\right.\\
\left.:(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{n})\in\overline{B_{ns}(0,1)}\right\}

and for
(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{n})\in\overline{B(0,1)}_{ns}
(here,
\overline{B(0,1)}_{ns}
denotes the closed unit ball in
{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times ns}
) with
\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{n}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
, there exists a unique element
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\eta^{0}_{1}&\cdots&\eta^{0}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]_{\nu}\in\mathcal{N}^{\oplus n}
such that

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\nu_{1}&\cdots&\nu_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right](\!(\overline{H}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}-\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\bar{\eta}_{1}&\cdots&\bar{\eta}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}:\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\eta_{1}&\cdots&\eta_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathcal{N}^{\oplus n}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\nu_{1}&\cdots&\nu_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right](\!(\overline{H}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}-\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\bar{\eta}^{0}_{1}&\cdots&\bar{\eta}^{0}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]_{\nu}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}.

Thus, it remains to prove that

\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\bar{\eta}^{0}_{1}&\cdots&\bar{\eta}^{0}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]_{\nu}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\nu_{1}&\cdots&\nu_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right](\!(\overline{N}^{0}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}

for some
(\!(N^{0}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}
in
(\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1})^{n\times n}
.

Let
P:\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}^{\perp}
be the orthogonal projection and
P^{(n)}:(\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s})^{\oplus n}\rightarrow(\mathcal{N}^{\perp})^{\oplus n}
be the amplification of
P
defined by

P^{(n)}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}H_{1}&\cdots&H_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}P(H_{1})&\cdots&P(H_{n})\end{smallmatrix}\right].

Let
\overline{P}
and
\overline{P^{(n)}}
be the orthogonal projections between the corresponding conjugated spaces, and note that
\overline{P}^{(n)}=\overline{P^{(n)}}
.
Then observe that

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\nu_{1}&\cdots&\nu_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right](\!(\overline{H}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}-\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\bar{\eta}_{1}&\cdots&\bar{\eta}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}:\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\eta_{1}&\cdots&\eta_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\mathcal{N}^{\oplus n}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\overline{P}^{(n)}\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\nu_{1}&\cdots&\nu_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right](\!(\overline{H}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}\right)}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\overline{P}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\overline{H}_{k1}\nu_{k}\right)&\cdots&\overline{P}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\overline{H}_{kn}\nu_{k}\right)\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\overline{P}(\overline{H}_{k1}\nu_{k})&\cdots&\sum_{k=1}^{n}\overline{P}(\overline{H}_{kn}\nu_{k})\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\overline{H}_{k1}\nu_{k}-\overline{N}^{0}_{k1}\nu_{k})&\cdots&\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\overline{H}_{kn}\nu_{k}-\overline{N}^{0}_{kn}\nu_{k}\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\nu_{1}&\cdots&\nu_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\left[(\!(\overline{H}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}-(\!(\overline{N}^{0}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}

where the second last equality holds from part (i) and, for each
l,k=1,\ldots,n
,
N^{0}_{lk}
is the unique element in
\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}
such that

\inf\left\{\left\|{H_{lk}-N_{lk}}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:N_{lk}\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}=\left\|{H_{lk}-N^{0}_{lk}}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}.

Consequently, by the uniqueness of
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\eta^{0}_{1}&\cdots&\eta^{0}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]_{\nu}
we have that

\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\bar{\eta}^{0}_{1}&\cdots&\bar{\eta}^{0}_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right]_{\nu}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\nu_{1}&\cdots&\nu_{n}\end{smallmatrix}\right](\!(\overline{N}^{0}_{lk})\!)_{l,k=1}^{n}

verifying the claim and hence, it completes the proof of part (ii) as in part (i).

(iii) Let
H^{1},H^{2}\in\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}
and
Q(H^{l})=H^{l}-N^{l}
where
N^{l}\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}
are unique elements such that

\inf\left\{\left\|{H^{l}-N}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}=\left\|{H^{l}-N^{l}}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}},~l=1,2.

Observe that it is enough to show for
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in{\mathbb{C}}
, that

\inf\left\{\left\|{\lambda_{1}H^{1}+\lambda_{2}H^{2}-N}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}=\left\|{\lambda_{1}[H^{1}-N^{1}]+\lambda_{2}[H^{2}-N^{2}]}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}.

For
\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
, recall from the proof of part (i) that there exists unique
N_{0}\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}
satisfying

( 3.14 )

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\left\|{\lambda_{1}H^{1}+\lambda_{2}H^{2}-N}\right\|_{s,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:N\in\mathcal{N}^{s\times 1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\sup\left\{\left\|{\mu[\bar{\lambda}_{1}\overline{H}^{1}+\bar{\lambda}_{2}\overline{H}^{2}-\overline{N}_{0}]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}}:\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s},\left\|{\mu}\right\|\leq 1\right\}.

Also, note that for any
\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
,

\left\|{\mu[\bar{\lambda}_{1}\overline{H}^{1}+\bar{\lambda}_{2}\overline{H}^{2}-\overline{N}_{0}]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}}=\left\|{\mu[\bar{\lambda}_{1}(\overline{H}^{1}-\overline{N}^{1})]+\mu[\bar{\lambda}_{2}(\overline{H}^{2}-\overline{N}^{2})}\right\|.

since
\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}
is a Hilbert space. Consequently, the proof of part (iii) follows from Equation (
3.14
)
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{K}
be Hilbert spaces over
{\mathbb{C}}
equipped with the row operator space structure – denoted by
\mathcal{H}_{r}
and
\mathcal{K}_{r}
, respectively. Let
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K})
be the space of bounded linear operators from
\mathcal{H}
to
\mathcal{K}
equipped with the natural operator space structure on it. For any
s\in\mathbb{N}
, assume that an element
T=(\!(T_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{s}
in
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H},\mathcal{K})^{s\times s}
has closed range as a linear mapping
T:\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\oplus s}
. Then there exists
C>0
such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
H=(\!(H_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathcal{H}_{r}^{ns\times ns}
with
H_{ij}\in\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}
,
i,j=1,\ldots,n
,

\left\|{R_{T^{\oplus n}}(Q^{\oplus n}(H))}\right\|_{ns,\mathcal{K}_{r}}\geq C\left\|{Q^{\oplus n}(H)}\right\|_{ns,\mathcal{H}_{r}}

where
Q:\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}
is the linear mapping defined in (iii) in Lemma
3.6
with
N=\ker R_{T^{\oplus n}}
. Here,
R_{T}:\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}_{r}^{s\times s}
is defined by the right multiplication by
T
.

Proof.

First, observe that
\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}\cong\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times s}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\oplus s}\cong\mathcal{K}_{r}^{1\times s}
. Since
T:\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\oplus s}
acting from right on the row vectors over
\mathcal{H}
has closed range, there exists
C>0
such that for
h\in\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}
,

( 3.15 )

\left\|{T(h)}\right\|_{\mathcal{K}^{\oplus s}}\geq C\left\|{[h]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}/\ker T}.

Also, for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!h_{1}&\cdots&h_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]T^{\oplus n}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathcal{K}_{r}^{1\times ns}}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\|{T(h_{j})}\right\|^{2}_{\mathcal{K}^{\oplus s}}\geq C^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\|{[h_{j}]}\right\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}/\ker T}\\
=C^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\inf\left\{\left\|{h_{j}-n_{j}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}}:n_{j}\in\ker T\right\}=C^{2}\inf\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\|{h_{j}-n_{j}}\right\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}}:n_{j}\in\ker T\right\}\\
=C^{2}\inf\left\{\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!h_{1}&\cdots&h_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]-\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!\eta_{1}&\cdots&\eta_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}:\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!\eta_{1}&\cdots&\eta_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\ker T^{\oplus n}\right\}=C^{2}\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!h_{1}&\cdots&h_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}/\ker T^{\oplus n}}

where the second equality holds from Equation (
3.15
).

Now for
H=(\!(H_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathcal{H}_{r}^{ns\times ns}
, we compute

\left\|{R_{T^{\oplus n}}(Q^{\oplus n}(H))}\right\|_{ns,\mathcal{K}_{r}}=\sup\left\{\left\|{\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!v_{1}&\cdots&v_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]\overline{H}\right)T^{\oplus n}}\right\|_{\mathcal{K}_{r}^{1\times ns}}:(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in\overline{B(0,1)}_{ns}\right\}\\
\geq C\sup\left\{\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!v_{1}&\cdots&v_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]\overline{H}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}/\ker T^{\oplus n}}:(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in\overline{B(0,1)}_{ns}\right\}\\
=C\sup\!\left\{\inf\!\left\{\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!v_{1}&\cdots&v_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]\overline{H}-\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!\bar{\eta}_{1}&\cdots&\bar{\eta}_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}:\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!\eta_{1}&\cdots&\eta_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]\in\ker T^{\oplus n}\right\}:\right.\\
\left.(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in\overline{B(0,1)}_{ns}\right\}=C\sup\left\{\!\inf\!\left\{\!\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!v_{1}&\cdots&v_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]\overline{H}\!-\!\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\!v_{1}&\cdots&v_{n}\!\end{smallmatrix}\right]\overline{N}}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}}\!\!\!:\!N\in\ker R_{T^{\oplus n}}\right\}\!:\right.\\
\left.\!(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n})\in\!\overline{B(0,1)}_{ns}\!\right\}=C\inf\left\{\left\|{H-N}\right\|_{ns,\mathcal{H}_{r}}:N\in\ker R_{T^{\oplus n}}\right\}=C\left\|{Q^{\oplus n}(H)}\right\|_{ns,\mathcal{H}_{r}}

where the first equality holds from the definition of
Q^{\oplus n}:(\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s})^{n\times n}\rightarrow(\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s})^{n\times n}
and the last one is obtained by using Lemma
3.6
with
\mathcal{N}=\ker T
and taking account the fact that

\ker T^{\oplus n}=\underbrace{\ker T\oplus\cdots\oplus\ker T}_{n-\text{times}}\subset\mathcal{H}_{r}^{1\times ns}\quad\text{and}\quad\ker R_{T^{\oplus n}}=(\ker T^{\oplus n})^{ns\times 1}\subset\mathcal{H}_{r}^{ns\times ns},

n\in\mathbb{N}
. This completes the proof.
∎

We conclude this section with the following theorem – a special case of Theorem
3.4
in the case of row Hilbert operator space – which we will use later in Section
5
.

Theorem 3.8
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be an operator space, and a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
be equipped with the row operator space structure – denoted by
\mathcal{H}_{r}
. Let
\Omega\subset\mathcal{V}_{nc}
be a bounded, uniformly open nc set which is right admissible and fix
s\in\mathbb{N}
, a point
Y\in\Omega_{s}
so that
\mathcal{C}_{1}
is a complemented sub-bi-module of
\mathcal{V}^{s\times s}
over
\mathcal{C}(Y)
and
H\in\mathcal{H}_{r}^{s\times s}
. Consider uniformly analytic nc functions

\mathcal{T}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})_{nc}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{F}:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{H}_{r})_{nc}

satisfying
\mathcal{F}(X)\in\mathrm{ran}~\mathcal{T}(X)
for
X\in\Omega
and
R_{\mathcal{T}(Y)}(H)=\mathcal{F}(Y)
where
\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
is equipped with the natural operator space structure on it. Assume that
\mathcal{T}(Y):\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\oplus s}
satisfies the following condition: for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, if
X_{n}\in\Omega_{ms}
with
X_{n}
converging to
Y^{\oplus m}
,
K_{n}\in\mathrm{ran}~\mathcal{T}(X_{n})
and
K_{n}
converges to
K
, then
K\in\mbox{ran }\mathcal{T}(Y^{\oplus m})
(in particular,
\mathcal{T}(Y)^{\oplus m}
has closed range for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
). Then there exist a uniformly open nc neighbourhood
U
of
Y
and a uniformly analytic function
\mathcal{G}:U\rightarrow(\mathcal{H}_{r})_{nc}
such that

\mathcal{G}(Y)=H\quad\mbox{and}\quad R_{\mathcal{T}(X)}(\mathcal{G}(X))=\mathcal{F}(X),\quad\mbox{for all}~X\in U.

Proof.

The proof follows from Lemma
3.7
and Theorem
3.4
.
∎

4.
Noncommutative reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces

In this section, we explore fundamental properties of noncommutative reproducing kernels that will be used in the subsequent sections. We begin by revisiting the concept of completely positive (cp) noncommutative (nc) reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces and their complete characterization, as presented in
[
10
]
. Following this, we examine the nc space constructed over such a Hilbert space, where we introduce a notion of
generalized kernel elements
. Finally, we investigate the Hilbert
C^{*}
module structure on the matrix spaces over a cp nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space.

4.1.
Global and noncommutative kernels

Following
[
10
]
, we begin this subsection by reviewing the definitions and some elementary properties of nc kernels. We then examine the behavior of the derivatives of nc kernels in Proposition
4.4
, where it is shown that, unlike the classical case, the derivative of a nc kernel element remains a kernel element.

In what follows, we consider operator spaces
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{W}_{0},\mathcal{W}_{1}
, and use the notation

\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{W}_{0,nc},\mathcal{W}_{1,nc}):=\coprod_{m,n\geq 1}\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{W}_{0}^{n\times m},\mathcal{W}_{1}^{n\times m}).

4.1.1.
Global kernels

A
global kernel
on an uniformly open subset
\Omega
of
\mathcal{V}_{nc}
is a function
K:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{W}_{0,nc},\mathcal{W}_{1,nc})
satisfying following two conditions:

•

K
is graded: For all
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,

( 4.1 )

K(Z,W)\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{W}_{0}^{n\times m},\mathcal{W}_{1}^{n\times m}),\quad\text{whenever}\quad Z\in\Omega_{n},W\in\Omega_{m};

•

K
respects direct sums: For all
n_{1},n_{2},m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and
Z_{i}\in\Omega_{i}
with
i=1,2
,
W_{j}\in\Omega_{j}
with
j=1,2
,
P_{ij}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{i}\times m_{j}}
with
i,j=1,2
,

( 4.2 )

K\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}Z_{1}&0\\
0&Z_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}W_{1}&0\\
0&W_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}P_{11}&P_{12}\\
P_{21}&P_{22}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}K(Z_{1},W_{1})(P_{11})&K(Z_{1},W_{2})(P_{12})\\
K(Z_{2},W_{1})(P_{21})&K(Z_{2},W_{2})(P_{22})\end{smallmatrix}\right].

Note that the second condition above can be extended to evaluations of
K
on block diagonal matrices with arbitrary many blocks on the diagonal.

4.1.2.
Noncommutative kernels

Suppose that
K:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{W}_{0,nc},\mathcal{W}_{1,nc})
is a global kernel. Then
K
is said to be
nc affine kernel
if it respects the following
intertwining conditions
: For any
n,m,\widetilde{n},\widetilde{m}\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z\in\Omega_{n}
,
\widetilde{Z}\in\Omega_{\widetilde{n}}
,
\mathsf{A}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\widetilde{n}\times n}
such that
\mathsf{A}Z=\widetilde{Z}\mathsf{A}
, and
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
\widetilde{W}\in\Omega_{\widetilde{m}}
,
\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times\widetilde{m}}
such that
W\mathsf{B}=\mathsf{B}\widetilde{W}
,

( 4.3 )

\mathsf{A}K(Z,W)(P)\mathsf{B}=K(\widetilde{Z},\widetilde{W})(\mathsf{A}P\mathsf{B}),\quad\text{for all}~~P\in\mathcal{W}_{0}^{n\times m}.

Or equivalently, a global kernel
K
as above is a nc affine kernel if it respects the similarities in the following sense: For any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z,\widetilde{Z}\in\Omega_{n}
,
\mathsf{A}\in\text{GL}(n,{\mathbb{C}})
such that
\widetilde{Z}=\mathsf{A}Z\mathsf{A}^{-1}
, and
W,\widetilde{W}\in\Omega_{m}
,
\mathsf{B}\in\text{GL}(m,{\mathbb{C}})
such that
\widetilde{W}=\mathsf{B}^{-1}W\mathsf{B}
,

( 4.4 )

K(\widetilde{Z},\widetilde{W})(P)=\mathsf{A}K(Z,W)(\mathsf{A}^{-1}P\mathsf{B}^{-1})\mathsf{B},\quad\text{for all}~~P\in\mathcal{W}_{0}^{n\times m}.

Observe from the definition of nc functions, for any nc function
f:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{nc}
, the right difference-differential operator
\Delta_{R}f:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V}_{nc},\mathcal{W}_{nc})
turns out to be a nc affine kernel (cf.
[
36
, Proposition 2.15]
).

In the present article, our main interest, however, is in a variant of the affine kernels, which we call
nc kernels
. A
nc kernel
is a function
K:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{W}_{0,nc},\mathcal{W}_{1,nc})
satisfying

•

K
is
graded
in the sense of Equation (
4.1
) and

•

K
respects
intertwinings
, namely, for any
n,m,\widetilde{n},\widetilde{m}\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z\in\Omega_{n}
,
\widetilde{Z}\in\Omega_{\widetilde{n}}
,
\mathsf{A}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\widetilde{n}\times n}
such that
\mathsf{A}Z=\widetilde{Z}\mathsf{A}
, and
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
\widetilde{W}\in\Omega_{\widetilde{m}}
,
\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times\widetilde{m}}
such that
\mathsf{B}W=\widetilde{W}\mathsf{B}
,

( 4.5 )

\mathsf{A}K(Z,W)(P)\mathsf{B}^{*}=K(\widetilde{Z},\widetilde{W})(\mathsf{A}P\mathsf{B}^{*}),\quad\text{for all}~~P\in\mathcal{W}_{0}^{n\times m}.

Or equivalently,

•

K
is
graded
in the sense of Equation (
4.1
),

•

K
respects
direct sums
in the sense of Equation (
4.2
), and

•

for any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z,\widetilde{Z}\in\Omega_{n}
,
\mathsf{A}\in\text{GL}(n,{\mathbb{C}})
such that
\widetilde{Z}=\mathsf{A}Z\mathsf{A}^{-1}
, and
W,\widetilde{W}\in\Omega_{m}
,
\mathsf{B}\in\text{GL}(m,{\mathbb{C}})
such that
\widetilde{W}=\mathsf{B}W\mathsf{B}^{-1}
,

( 4.6 )

K(\widetilde{Z},\widetilde{W})(P)=\mathsf{A}K(Z,W)(\mathsf{A}^{-1}P(\mathsf{B}^{-1})^{*})\mathsf{B}^{*},\quad\text{for all}~~P\in\mathcal{W}_{0}^{n\times m}.

Notice that if
K
is nc affine kernel then the mapping
(X,Y)\mapsto K(X,Y^{*})
is a nc kernel.

4.1.3.
Completely positive global or nc kernels

First, note that we need an order structure on the operators spaces
\mathcal{W}_{0,nc}
and
\mathcal{W}_{1,nc}
to talk about positivity of any elements in these spaces. Recall that a
concrete operator system

\mathcal{S}
is a self-adjoint linear subspace of
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
for some Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
containing the identity operator
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}
. Any concrete operator system
\mathcal{S}
, as well as, for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
the space
\mathcal{S}^{n\times n}
of
n\times n
matrices over
\mathcal{S}
are equipped with a special cone – the cone of poistive semi-definite elements – inducing an ordering on
\mathcal{S}^{n\times n}
satisfying certain compatibility conditions (cf.
[
43
, pp. 176]
). Any abstract operator space with such a distinguished cone satisfying those compatibility conditions is called an
abstract operator system
. The Choi-Effros Theorem (cf.
[
43
, Theorem 13.1]
) shows that any such abstract operator system is completely order isomorphic to a concrete operator system.

In this subsection, we assume that
\mathcal{W}_{0,nc}
and
\mathcal{W}_{1,nc}
are abstract operator systems equipped with an associated conjugate-linear involution
P\mapsto P^{*}
and an well-defined notion of positivity
P\succeq 0
.

A nc kernel
K:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{W}_{0,nc},\mathcal{W}_{1,nc})
with
\Omega\subset\mathcal{V}_{nc}
is a
completely positive noncommutative kernel
(cp nc kernel) if for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
Z\in\Omega_{n}
,

( 4.7 )

K(Z,Z)(P)\succeq 0~~\text{in}~~\mathcal{W}_{1}^{n\times n},\quad\text{for all}~~P\succeq 0~~\text{in}~~\mathcal{W}_{0}^{n\times n}.

Whenever
K
is a global kernel satisfying Equation (
4.7
), we say
K
is a
cp global kernel
. In case, both
\mathcal{W}_{0,nc}
as well as
\mathcal{W}_{1,nc}
are
C^{*}
-algebras, we have the following equivalent condition for completely positivity of a kernel function.

Proposition 4.1
.

[
10
, Proposition 2.2]

The global or nc kernel
K:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{W}_{0,nc},\mathcal{W}_{1,nc})
is cp if and only if for any
Z^{j}\in\Omega_{n_{j}}
,
P_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{0}^{N\times n_{j}}
,
Q_{j}\in\mathcal{W}_{1}^{n_{j}}
for
n_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
,
1\leq j\leq N
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds that

\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}Q_{i}^{*}K(Z^{i},Z^{j})(P_{i}^{*}P_{j})Q_{j}\succeq 0.

4.1.4.
Characterization of noncommutative kernels

We now present the result cahracterizing cp nc kernels assuming that
\mathcal{W}_{0}
is a
C^{*}
-algebra and
\mathcal{W}_{1,nc}
is the concrete operator system consisting of all bounded linear operators on a Hilbert space.

Theorem 4.2
.

[
10
, Theorem 3.1]

Suppose that
\mathcal{V}
is an operator space,
\Omega\subset\mathcal{V}_{nc}
is a nc set,
\mathcal{Y}
is a Hilbert space,
\mathcal{A}
is a
C^{*}
-algebra and
K:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{nc},\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{Y})_{nc})
is a given function. Then the following are equivalent.

(1)

K
is a cp nc kernel.

(2)

There is a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}(K)
whose elements are nc functions
f:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Y})_{nc}
such that

(a)

For each
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
v\in\mathcal{A}^{1\times m}
, and
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{m\times 1}
, the function

K_{W,v,y}:\Omega_{n}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Y})^{n\times n}\cong\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}^{n\times 1},\mathcal{Y}^{n\times 1})~~~\text{defined by}

( 4.8 )

K_{W,v,y}(Z)(u):=K(Z,W)(uv)y,

for all
Z\in\Omega_{n}
,
u\in\mathcal{A}^{n\times 1}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}(K)
.

(b)

The kernel elements
K_{W,v,y}
as defined in Equation (
4.8
) have the reproducing property: For
f\in\mathcal{H}(K)
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
v\in\mathcal{A}^{1\times m}
and
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{m\times 1}
,

( 4.9 )

\left\langle f,K_{W,v,y}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}(K)}=\left\langle f(W)(v^{*}),y\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Y}^{m\times 1}}.

(c)

\mathcal{H}(K)
is equipped with a unital
*
-representation
\rho
mapping
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H}(K))
such that

( 4.10 )

(\rho(a)f)(W)(v^{*})=f(W)(v^{*}a)

for
a\in\mathcal{A}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
v\in\mathcal{A}^{1\times m}
, with the action on the kernel elements given by

( 4.11 )

\rho(a):K_{W,v,y}\mapsto K_{W,av,y}.

(3)

K
has a Kolmogorov decomposition: there is a Hilbert space
\mathcal{X}
equipped with a unital
*
-representation
\rho:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{X})
together with a nc function
H:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})_{nc}
such that

( 4.12 )

K(Z,W)(P)=H(Z)(\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times m}}\otimes\rho)(P)H(W)^{*}

for all
Z\in\Omega_{n}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
P\in\mathcal{A}^{n\times m}
.

Furthermore, if
\mathcal{H}(K)
consists of uniformly nc analytic functions on
\Omega
taking values in
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Y})_{nc}
, then
K
turns out to be uniformly
sesqui-analytic
nc function of order
1
as demonstrated in the following proposition. So we first provide the definition of
uniformly anti-analytic nc function
.

Definition 4.3
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathcal{V}_{nc}
be a nc domain which is both left and right admissible and
\mathcal{W}
be a complex vector space. Then a nc function
f:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{nc}
is said to be uniformly anti-analytic nc function if the function
f_{*}:\Omega^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{nc}
defined by

f_{*}(W):=f(W^{*})

is uniformly analytic nc function on
\Omega
where
\Omega^{*}:=\{W:W^{*}\in\Omega\}
. In this case, we define

{}_{*}\Delta_{R}f(W^{0},W^{1})(X):=\Delta_{R}f_{*}((W^{0})^{*},(W^{1})^{*})(X^{*}),

for
W^{0}\in\Omega_{n_{0}},W^{1}\in\Omega_{n_{1}},X\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{0}\times n_{1}}
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathcal{V}_{nc}
and
\mathcal{H}(K)
be a nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space with the cp nc reproducing kernel
K:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{nc},\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{Y})_{nc})
. Assume that
\mathcal{H}(K)
consists of
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Y})_{nc}
valued uniformly analytic nc functions on
\Omega
, and that
\Omega
is both left and right admissible. Denote
\Omega^{*}=\{W:W^{*}\in\Omega\}
. Then

(i)

K
is a uniformly sesqui-analytic nc function of order
1
on
\Omega\times\Omega
. In other words, the function
K_{*}:\Omega\times\Omega^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{nc},\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{Y})_{nc})
defined by

K_{*}(Z,W):=K(Z,W^{*}),~~~Z\in\Omega,~~W\in\Omega^{*},

is a uniformly analytic nc function of order
1
.

(ii)

For any
W^{0}\in\Omega_{n_{0}},\ldots,W^{\ell}\in\Omega_{n_{\ell}}
,
X^{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{0}\times n_{1}},\ldots,X^{\ell}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{\ell-1}\times n_{\ell}}
,
v\in\mathcal{A}^{1\times n_{0}}
and
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{n_{\ell}\times 1}
,

{}_{1^{*}}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W^{0},\ldots,W^{\ell})((\cdot)v,X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})y\in\mathcal{H}(K)

whenever

\begin{bmatrix}W^{0}&X^{1}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&W^{1}&X^{2}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&0\\
0&0&\cdots&W^{\ell-1}&X^{\ell}\\
0&0&\cdots&0&W^{\ell}\end{bmatrix}\in\Omega_{n_{0}+\cdots+n_{\ell}}.

Furthermore,

{}_{1^{*}}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W^{0},\ldots,W^{\ell})((\cdot)v,X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})y=K_{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}W^{0}&0&0&\cdots&0\\
X^{1}&W^{1}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&X^{2}&W^{2}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\ldots&X^{\ell}&W^{\ell}\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}v&0&\cdots&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
\vdots\\
0\\
y\end{smallmatrix}\right]}.

(iii)

For every
f\in\mathcal{H}(K)
,
W^{0}\in\Omega_{n_{0}},\ldots,W^{\ell}\in\Omega_{n_{\ell}}
,
X^{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{0}\times n_{1}},\ldots,X^{\ell}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{\ell-1}\times n_{\ell}}
,
v\in\mathcal{A}^{1\times n_{0}}
and
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{n_{\ell}\times 1}
,

( 4.13 )

\displaystyle\left\langle f,_{1^{*}}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W^{0},\ldots,W^{\ell})((\cdot)v,X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})y\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}(K)}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Delta_{R}^{\ell}f(W^{\ell},W^{1},\ldots,W^{\ell-1},W^{0})(X^{\ell},X^{2},\ldots,X^{\ell-1},X^{1})v^{*},y\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Y}^{n_{\ell}}}.

Proof.

(i) We begin by pointing out that since
\mathcal{H}(K)
consists of
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Y})_{nc}
valued uniformly analytic nc functions on
\Omega
,
K
is uniformly
P
-locally bounded ( cf.
[
10
, Theorem 3.17]
) and hence the proof follows from Theorem 7.53 in
[
36
, Chapter 7, pp 120]
.

(ii) Since
K_{*}
is a uniformly analytic nc function of order
1
on
\Omega\times\Omega^{*}
, for
Z\in\Omega_{n}
,
W^{0}\in\Omega_{n_{0}}
,
W^{1}\in\Omega_{n_{1}}
,
X^{1}\in\mathcal{V}^{n_{0}\times n_{1}}
,
u\in\mathcal{A}^{n\times 1}
,
v\in\mathcal{A}^{1\times n_{0}}
and
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{n_{1}\times 1}
, recall (see
[
36
, Proposition 3.17, Equation (3.21), pp. 43]
) that

\Delta_{R}K_{*}(Z,(W^{0})^{*},(W^{1})^{*})(uv,(X^{1})^{*})y=K_{*}\left(Z,\left[\begin{smallmatrix}(W^{0})^{*}&(X^{1})^{*}\\
0&(W^{1})^{*}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\left([\begin{smallmatrix}uv&0\end{smallmatrix}]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}^{n_{1}\times n_{1}}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)y.

Consequently, we have that

\displaystyle\Delta_{R}K_{*}(Z,(W^{0})^{*},(W^{1})^{*})(uv,(X^{1})^{*})y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{*}\left(Z,\left[\begin{smallmatrix}(W^{0})^{*}&(X^{1})^{*}\\
0&(W^{1})^{*}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\left([\begin{smallmatrix}uv&0\end{smallmatrix}]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{Y}^{n_{1}\times n_{1}}}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{*}\left(Z,\left[\begin{smallmatrix}(W^{0})^{*}&(X^{1})^{*}\\
0&(W^{1})^{*}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\left([\begin{smallmatrix}uv&0\end{smallmatrix}]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
y\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K\left(Z,\left[\begin{smallmatrix}W^{0}&X^{1}\\
0&W^{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\left([\begin{smallmatrix}uv&0\end{smallmatrix}]\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
y\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}W^{0}&X^{1}\\
0&W^{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}v&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
y\end{smallmatrix}\right]}(Z)(u)

completing the proof of part (ii) for
\ell=1
and the same proof with matrices of larger sizes yields the general case.

(iii) As in the part (ii) we demonstrate the proof of part (iii) only for
\ell=1
and the general case follows directly. Recall from Theorem
4.2
that, for any
f\in\mathcal{H}(K)
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
v\in\mathcal{A}^{1\times m}
,
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{m\times 1}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\langle f,K_{W,v,y}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}(K)}=\langle f(W)(v^{*}),y\rangle_{\mathcal{Y}^{m}}.

Consequently, this reproducing property together with part (ii) yield that

\displaystyle\left\langle f,_{1^{*}}\Delta_{R}^{l}K(\cdot,W^{0},W^{1})((\cdot)v,X^{1})y\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}(K)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle f,K_{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}W^{0}&0\\
X^{1}&W^{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}v&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
y\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}(K)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle f\left(\left[\begin{smallmatrix}W^{0}&0\\
X^{1}&W^{1}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right)\left[\begin{smallmatrix}v^{*}\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
y\end{smallmatrix}\right]\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}(K)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\Delta_{L}f(W^{0},W^{1})(X^{1})v^{*},y\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Y}^{n_{1}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\Delta_{R}f(W^{1},W^{0})(X^{1})v^{*},y\right\rangle_{\mathcal{Y}^{n_{1}}}

where the last equality follows from Equation (
2.6
).
∎

Let
\mathcal{Y}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{2}
be two Hilbert spaces,
K_{i}:\Omega\times\Omega\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{nc},(\mathcal{Y}_{i})_{nc})
,
i=1,2
, be two cp nc kernels and
S:\Omega\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{Y}_{1},\mathcal{Y}_{2})
be a global function. Then following
[
10
, Section 4]
, we define
S
to be a
multiplier
if the operator
M_{S}:\mathcal{H}(K_{1})\to\mathcal{H}(K_{2})
defined by

( 4.14 )

(M_{S}(f))(W)=S(W)f(W)

is bounded. Using closed graph theorem it can be seen that
M_{S}
is bounded whenever it is well defined. Moreover, the boundedness of
M_{S}
can be expressed as the completely positivity condition of certain nc kernels as demonstrated in the following result.

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
K_{i}:\Omega\times\Omega\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{nc},(\mathcal{Y}_{i})_{nc})
,
i=1,2
, be two cp nc kernels and
S:\Omega\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{Y}_{1},\mathcal{Y}_{2})
be a global function. Then the following are equivalent.

(1)

The operator
M_{S}
is bounded.

(2)

There exists
C\geq 0
such that the kernel function
K_{S}:\Omega\times\Omega\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{nc},(\mathcal{Y}_{2})_{nc})
defined by

K_{S}(Z,W)(P):=CK_{1}(Z,W)(P)-S(Z)K_{2}(Z,W)(P)S(W)^{*}

is a cp nc kernel.

Moreover, if one these two happens, the norm
\left\|{M_{S}}\right\|
turns out to be the infimum of all positive constants
C
satisfying the condition (2).

Proof.

Since the proof is verbatim to the proof of Theorem 4.1 in
[
10
]
with an exception that
\left\|{M_{S}}\right\|
is assumed to be at most
1
there, we omit it here.
∎

If
M_{S}:\mathcal{H}(K_{1})\to\mathcal{H}(K_{2})
as in the theorem above is a bounded linear operator, then the action of its adjoint
(M_{S})^{*}:\mathcal{H}(K_{2})\to\mathcal{H}(K_{1})
on any kernel element
(K_{1})_{W,v,y}
,
W\in\Omega_{m},v\in\mathcal{A}^{1\times m}
,
y\in\mathcal{Y}^{m\times 1}
, turns out to be

( 4.15 )

(M_{S})^{*}((K_{1})_{W,v,y})=(K_{2})_{W,v,S(W)^{*}y}.

Let
K:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{nc},\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{Y})_{nc})
be a cp nc kernel and
\boldsymbol{M}=(M_{Z_{1}},\ldots,M_{Z_{d}})
be the
d
-tuple of multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions on the space of all nc functions on
\Omega
taking values in the nc space over
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Y})
defined by
f\mapsto M_{Z_{i}}(f)
and
M_{Z_{i}}(f)(W)=W_{i}f(W)
,
W\in\Omega
. Then as a corollary of the preceding theorem, we have that the operator
M_{Z_{j}}
defines a bounded linear operator on
\mathcal{H}(K)
if and only if there exists
C\geq 0
such that

K^{(j)}(Z,W)(P):=CK_{1}(Z,W)(P)-Z_{j}K_{2}(Z,W)(P)W_{j}^{*}

defines a cp nc kernel on
\Omega
. Furthermore, we have the following corollary.

Corollary 4.6
.

A nc function
f:\Omega\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Y})_{nc}
is in
\mathcal{H}(K)
if and only if there exists
C\geq 0
such that

K_{f}(Z,W)(P):=CK(Z,W)(P)-f(Z)Pf(W)^{*}

defines a cp nc kernel on
\Omega
.

4.2.
Noncommutative space over
\mathcal{H}(K)

We now study the nc space over
\mathcal{H}(K)
when
\mathcal{A}
is a unital
C^{*}
algebra with the unit element
\mathsf{e}
and
\mathcal{Y}={\mathbb{C}}^{r}
, that is, the nc cp kernel
K
is now a function
K:\Omega\times\Omega\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{nc},{\mathbb{C}}^{r\times r}_{nc})
and in consequence, the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}(K)
consists of nc functions on
\Omega
taking values in
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},{\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc}
. Recall that the nc space
\mathcal{H}(K)_{nc}
over
\mathcal{H}(K)
is, by definition (see Equation (
2.1
)), the graded space

\mathcal{H}(K)_{nc}:=\coprod_{m\geq 1}\mathcal{H}(K)^{m\times m}.

For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the space
\mathcal{H}(K)^{m\times m}
of
m\times m
matrices over
\mathcal{H}(K)
– which we denote by
\mathcal{H}_{m}
– carries a canonical bi-module structure over
{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
, namely, for
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},\mathsf{S}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
and
f=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})\in\mathcal{H}(K)
,

\mathsf{A}(f\otimes\mathsf{S})\mathsf{B}=f\otimes(\mathsf{A}\mathsf{S}\mathsf{B}).

Moreover, the evaluation of an element
f\otimes\mathsf{S}=(f_{1}\otimes\mathsf{S},\ldots,f_{r}\otimes\mathsf{S})\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
at any point
W\in\Omega_{m}
can be thought of as a linear mapping on
\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
taking values in
({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m})^{r}
as follows

\left[f(W)\otimes\mathsf{S}\right](\mathtt{A})=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}f_{1}(W)\mathtt{A}\mathsf{S}\\
\vdots\\
f_{r}(W)\mathtt{A}\mathsf{S}\end{smallmatrix}\right],~~~\mathtt{A}=(\!(a_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m},

where the linear map
f_{j}(W):\mathcal{A}^{m\times 1}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1}
acts column wise on
\mathtt{A}
. Finally, recalling the action of
\mathcal{A}
on the kernel elements in (
4.11
), we point out that there is a canonical action of
\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
on
\mathcal{H}_{m}
for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
defined as follows:

( 4.16 )

\mathtt{A}\cdot(\!(h_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{m}:=\bigg(\!\!\!\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\rho(a_{ik})h_{kj}\bigg)\!\!\!\bigg)_{i,j=1}^{m},~~~\mathtt{A}=(\!(a_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{m}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m},~~~(\!(h_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{m}\in\mathcal{H}_{m}.

In other words,
\mathcal{H}_{m}
turns out to be a left module over
\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

4.2.1.
Generalized kernel elements

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
and any vector
\sigma\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r\times 1}
, define the uniformly analytic nc functions
K(\cdot,W)\sigma:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}^{m},({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{m})_{nc}
by

( 4.17 )

K(\cdot,W)\sigma:=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}K_{W,\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\sigma}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j}

where
\{\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{m}\}
is the standard ordered basis for
{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times m}
. Note that
K(\cdot,W)\sigma
is an
m\times m
matrix over
\mathcal{H}(K)
whose
ij
-th entry is the nc function
K_{W,\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\sigma}
on
\Omega
taking values in
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},{\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc}
. We call these vectors
\{K(\cdot,W)\sigma\in\mathcal{H}_{m}:\sigma\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r\times 1}\}
for
W\in\Omega_{m}
as the
generalized kernel elements
. For every
\sigma\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r\times 1}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
\mathsf{A}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times m}
and
\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times t}
, observe that
\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma]\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{H}(K)^{s\times t}
where by
\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma]\mathsf{B}
we mean that

\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma]\mathsf{B}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}K_{W,\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\sigma}\otimes\mathsf{A}(\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j})\mathsf{B}.

On the other hand, the expression for
\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma]\mathsf{B}
has following equivalent presentation

\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma]\mathsf{B}=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\sum_{j=1}^{t}K_{W,\mathsf{e}\otimes e_{i}\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\epsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\sigma}\otimes e_{i}^{*}\epsilon_{j},

which in turn implies that

( 4.18 )

K_{W,\mathsf{e}\otimes e_{\ell}\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\epsilon_{k}^{*}\otimes\sigma}=e_{\ell}(\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma]\mathsf{B})\epsilon_{k}^{*},\quad 1\leq\ell\leq s,~~~1\leq k\leq t,~~~\sigma\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}.

Here,
\{e_{1},\ldots e_{s}\}
and
\{\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{t}\}
are the standard ordered bases for
{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times s}
and
{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times t}
, respectively. The left
\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
module action on the generalized kernel elements turns out to be

( 4.19 )

\mathtt{A}\cdot K(\cdot,W)\sigma=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}K_{W,(\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i})\mathtt{A},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\sigma}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j},~~~\mathtt{A}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}.

Likewise the kernel elements (see (
4.8
)), the generalized kernel elements give rise to nc functions on
\Omega
as shown in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\Omega^{*}=\{W:W^{*}\in\Omega\}
and
\sigma\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
be an arbitrary but fixed vector. Then the function
\gamma:\Omega^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}(K)_{nc}
defined by
\gamma(W):=K(\cdot,W^{*})\sigma
is uniformly analytic nc function.

Proof.

We begin by pointing out from the definition of
K(\cdot,W^{*})\sigma
in (
4.17
) that
\gamma
is a graded function. Next, we check that
\gamma
satisfies the intertwining. Let
W\in\Omega^{*}_{n},\widetilde{W}\in\Omega^{*}_{m}
and
\mathsf{S}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times n}
so that
\mathsf{S}W=\widetilde{W}\mathsf{S}
. Also, denote the standard ordered bases for
{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times n}
and
{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times m}
as
\{\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n}\}
and
\{\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{m}\}
, respectively. Then note that

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}K_{W^{*},\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i},\epsilon_{j}^{*}}\otimes\mathsf{S}\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\epsilon_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}K_{W^{*},\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}\mathsf{S},\epsilon_{j}^{*}}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\epsilon_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}K_{\widetilde{W}^{*},\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i},\mathsf{S}\epsilon_{j}^{*}}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\epsilon_{j}

where the second equality holds since

\displaystyle K_{W^{*},\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}\mathsf{S},\epsilon_{j}^{*}}(Z)u=K(Z,W^{*})(u(\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}\mathsf{S}))\epsilon_{j}^{*}

\displaystyle=K(Z,\widetilde{W}^{*})(u(\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}))\mathsf{S}\epsilon_{j}^{*}=K_{\widetilde{W}^{*},\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i},\mathsf{S}\epsilon_{j}^{*}}(Z)u,

for any
Z\in\Omega_{s}
,
u\in\mathcal{A}^{s\times 1}
and
s\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus it verifies that

\mathsf{S}\gamma(W)=\gamma(\widetilde{W})\mathsf{S}.

Finally, uniformly analyticity of
\gamma
as a nc function follows from part (i) in Proposition
4.4
.
∎

We now discuss the reproducing property of these
generalized kernel elements
for which we equip the space
\mathcal{H}_{m}
of
m\times m
matrices over
\mathcal{H}(K)
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
, with an hermitian product making it a Hilbert space as follows. For
F=(\!(f_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{m},\,G=(\!(g_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{m}\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
, define

( 4.20 )

\left\langle F,G\right\rangle_{HS}:=\mbox{tr}\left(F\ast G^{\top}\right).

Here,
F\ast G
is an element in
\mathcal{H}_{m}
whose
ij
-th entry is defined as follows:

( 4.21 )

(F\ast G)_{ij}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\left\langle f_{i\ell},g_{\ell j}\right\rangle.

Equivalently, for any
f\otimes\mathsf{A},g\otimes\mathsf{B}\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
, the formula above yields that

\left\langle f\otimes\mathsf{A},g\otimes\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{HS}=\langle f,g\rangle_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\langle\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}},

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}
is the usual Hilbert-Schmidt inner product on
{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
\xi=(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{r})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r\times 1}
and
F=(F_{1},\ldots,F_{r})
in
\mathcal{H}_{m}
.

(i)

For any
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
,

( 4.22 )

\left\langle F,\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\xi]\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{HS}=\sum_{t=1}^{r}\overline{\xi}_{t}\left\langle F_{t}(W)(\mathsf{A}^{*}),\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}.

In particular, for the standard ordered basis
\{\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{r}\}
of
{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
, we have that

( 4.23 )

\left\langle F,\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma_{t}]\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{HS}=\left\langle F_{t}(W)(\mathsf{A}^{*}),\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}},~~~1\leq t\leq r.

(ii)

For
\mathtt{A}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
,
\mathsf{X}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
,

( 4.24 )

\left\langle F,\mathtt{A}[K(\cdot,W)\xi]\mathsf{X}\right\rangle_{HS}=\sum_{t=1}^{r}\overline{\xi}_{t}\left\langle F_{t}(W)(\mathtt{A}^{*}),\mathsf{X}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}.

Proof.

(i) It is enough to verify the identity given in Equation (
4.23
) for any elementary tensors
f\otimes\mathsf{S}\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
. Note that

\displaystyle\left\langle f\otimes\mathsf{S},\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\xi]\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{HS}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle f\otimes\mathsf{S},\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}K_{W,\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\xi}\otimes\mathsf{A}(\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j})\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{HS}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\left\langle f\otimes\mathsf{S},K_{W,\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\xi}\otimes\mathsf{A}(\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j})\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{HS}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\left[\left\langle f,K_{W,\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\xi}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\left\langle\mathsf{S},\mathsf{A}(\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j})\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\left[\left\langle f(W)(\varepsilon_{i}^{*}),\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\xi\right\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{m}}\left\langle\mathsf{A}^{*}\mathsf{S}\mathsf{B}^{*},\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{r}\overline{\xi}_{t}\left\langle f_{t}(W)(\varepsilon_{i}^{*}),\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m}}\left\langle\mathsf{A}^{*}\mathsf{S}\mathsf{B}^{*},\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{r}\overline{\xi}_{t}\operatorname{trace}(f_{t}(W)\mathsf{A}^{*}\mathsf{S}\mathsf{B}^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{r}\overline{\xi}_{t}\left\langle(f_{t}(W)\otimes\mathsf{S})(\mathsf{A}^{*}),\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}

completing the proof. The second statement follows directly from this computation with
\xi=\sigma_{t}
,
1\leq t\leq r
.

(ii) A similar computation with the help of Equation (
4.19
) yields the desired identity.
∎

Remark 4.9
.

(i) When
\mathcal{A}={\mathbb{C}}
, the linear span of the set of vectors

\{\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma_{t}]\mathsf{B}:W\in\Omega_{m},\,\,\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m},\,1\leq t\leq r\}

is dense in
\mathcal{H}_{m}
for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Indeed, it follows from Equation (
4.23
) that only zero vector is perpendicular to
\mathsf{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma_{t}]\mathsf{B}
for all
1\leq t\leq r
,
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
and
W\in\Omega_{m}
.

(ii) For a general unital
C^{*}
algebra
\mathcal{A}
, it follows from part (ii) of the proposition above that the set

\{\mathtt{A}[K(\cdot,W)\sigma_{t}]\mathsf{X}:W\in\Omega_{m},\,\,\mathtt{A}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m},\,\mathsf{X}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m},\,1\leq t\leq r\}

turns out to be a dense subset of
\mathcal{H}_{m}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

(iii) Using the proposition above we have that

\mathtt{A}K(\cdot,W)=0\quad\text{if and only if}\quad K(W,W)(\mathtt{A}^{*}\mathtt{A})=0

for all
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
\mathtt{A}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

4.2.2.
Adjoint of multiplication operators

In this subsection, we restrict ourselves to the case when
\mathcal{V}={\mathbb{C}}^{d}
and describe the action of the adjoint of the
d
-tuple of multiplication operators by nc coordinate functions on
\mathcal{H}_{m}
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Recall that
\boldsymbol{M}=(M_{Z_{1}},\ldots,M_{Z_{d}})
is the
d
-tuple of multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions on
\mathcal{H}(K)
with
M_{Z_{i}}:\mathcal{H}(K)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}(K)
defined by
f\mapsto M_{Z_{i}}(f)
and
M_{Z_{i}}(f)(W)=W_{i}f(W)
,
W\in\Omega
. Here, we mean
(W_{i}f_{1}(W),\ldots,W_{i}f_{r}(W))
by
W_{i}f(W)
for
i=1,\ldots,d
and
f=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})\in\mathcal{H}(K)
. Then
\boldsymbol{M}
gives rise to a linear operator
D_{\boldsymbol{M}^{*}-W^{*}}:\mathcal{H}_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{m}^{\oplus d}
for every
W\in\Omega_{m}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
defined by

D_{\boldsymbol{M}^{*}-W^{*}}:=(M^{*}_{Z_{1}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\otimes R_{W_{1}^{*}},\ldots,M^{*}_{Z_{d}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\otimes R_{W_{d}^{*}}).

For
1\leq j\leq d
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathtt{A}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
and
\mathsf{T}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
, we have that

(M^{*}_{Z_{j}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\otimes R_{W_{j}^{*}})(\mathtt{A}[K(\cdot,W)\xi]\mathsf{T})=\mathtt{A}(K(\cdot,W)\xi)[W_{j}^{*},\mathsf{T}].

Indeed,

\displaystyle M_{Z_{j}}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}(\mathtt{A}[K(\cdot,W)\xi]\mathsf{T})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{Z_{j}}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}\left(\sum_{i,k=1}^{m}K_{W,(\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i})\mathtt{A},\mathsf{T}\varepsilon_{k}^{*}\otimes\xi}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,k=1}^{m}K_{W,(\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i})\mathtt{A},W_{j}^{*}\mathsf{T}\varepsilon_{k}^{*}\otimes\xi}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathtt{A}[K(\cdot,W)\xi]W_{j}^{*}\mathsf{T};

and
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\otimes R_{W_{j}^{*}}(\mathtt{A}[K(\cdot,W)\xi]\mathsf{T})=\mathtt{A}[K(\cdot,W)\xi]\mathsf{T}W_{j}^{*}
. Here, the validation of the second equality is guaranteed by Equation (
4.15
). It thus verifies the following inclusion: For
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in\Omega_{m}
,

( 4.25 )

N_{W}:=\{\mathtt{A}K(\cdot,W)\xi:\mathtt{A}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m},\,\xi\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}\}\subset\ker D_{\boldsymbol{M}^{*}-W^{*}}.

Note that
N_{W}
is a left module over
\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
(in particular, over
{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
) for each
W\in\Omega_{m}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

As before, we now describe the action of
D_{\boldsymbol{M}^{*}-W^{*}}
on the derivatives of the
generalized kernel elements
. Since
Z\mapsto K(\cdot,Z)\xi
, for any
\xi\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
, is a uniformly anti-analytic nc function on
\Omega
taking values in
(\mathcal{H}_{r})_{nc}
, it follows, for
W^{0}\in\Omega_{n_{0}},\ldots,W^{\ell}\in\Omega_{n_{\ell}}
, that the function

{}_{1*}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W^{0},\ldots,W^{\ell})\xi:({\mathbb{C}}^{d})^{n_{0}\times n_{1}}\times\cdots\times({\mathbb{C}}^{d})^{n_{{\ell}-1}\times n_{\ell}}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{n_{0}\times n_{\ell}}

is a multi-linear mapping over
{\mathbb{C}}
. Consequently, in view of the identification made in Equation (
4.17
), when
n_{0}=\cdots=n_{\ell}=m
and
W\in\Omega_{m}
, we have that

( 4.26 )

(_{1*}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W,\ldots,W)\xi)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}K_{{}_{(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})}W^{\oplus{\ell}},\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\xi\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j}

where
{}_{(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})}W^{\oplus{\ell}}
denotes the point
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}W&0&0&\cdots&0\\
X^{1}&W&0&\cdots&0\\
0&X^{2}&W&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&X^{\ell}&W\end{smallmatrix}\right]
. In a similar manner as in Equation (
4.18
), we also have the following identities involving derivatives:

( 4.27 )

\mathsf{A}(_{1*}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W,\ldots,W)\xi)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})\mathsf{B}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}K_{{}_{(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})}W^{\oplus{\ell}},\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\xi\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\otimes\mathsf{A}(\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j})\mathsf{B},

or equivalently,

( 4.28 )

\mathsf{A}(_{1*}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W,\ldots,W)\xi)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})\mathsf{B}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}K_{{}_{(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})}W^{\oplus{\ell}},\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\varepsilon_{i}\mathsf{A}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
\mathsf{B}\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\otimes\xi\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j},

for
\xi\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r\times 1}
,
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
and
X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
. The last equation thus yields the action of
{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
on these derivatives of generalized kernel elements. Finally, the action of
\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
on these derivatives of generalized kernel elements show up as

( 4.29 )

\mathtt{A}(_{1*}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W,\ldots,W)\xi)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})\mathsf{B}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}K_{{}_{(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})}W^{\oplus{\ell}},\left[\begin{smallmatrix}(\mathsf{e}\otimes\varepsilon_{i})\mathtt{A}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right],\left[\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
\mathsf{B}\varepsilon_{j}\otimes\xi\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\otimes\varepsilon_{i}^{*}\varepsilon_{j}.

In this set-up, the following proposition yields the reproducing property of the
generalized kernel elements
as well as the action of
D_{\boldsymbol{M}^{*}-W^{*}}
on them.

Proposition 4.10
.

Let
\Omega\subset({\mathbb{C}}^{d})_{nc}
and
\mathcal{H}(K)\subset\mathcal{N}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}(\Omega,\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A},{\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc})
be a nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space with the nc cp reproducing kernel
K:\Omega\times\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{A}_{nc},({\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc})
. Assume that
\Omega
is both left and right admissible. Denote
\Omega^{*}=\{W:W^{*}\in\Omega\}
. Then

(i)

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
\mathtt{A}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
,
\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
,
X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell}\in({\mathbb{C}}^{d})^{m\times m}
,
F=(F_{1},\ldots,F_{r})\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
and the standard ordered basis
\{\sigma_{t}:1\leq t\leq r\}
of
{\mathbb{C}}^{r\times 1}
,

( 4.30 )

\displaystyle\left\langle F,\mathtt{A}\,(_{1*}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W,\ldots,W)\sigma_{t})(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})\,\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{HS}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Delta_{L}^{\ell}F_{t}(W,\ldots,W)(X^{\ell},X^{2},\ldots,X^{{\ell}-1},X^{1})(\mathtt{A}^{*}),\mathsf{B}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}},~~~t=1,\ldots,r.

(ii)

For every
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
\mathtt{A}\in\mathcal{A}^{m\times m}
,
\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
,
X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell}\in({\mathbb{C}}^{d})^{m\times m}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r\times 1}
and
1\leq k\leq d
,

( 4.31 )

\displaystyle\left(M_{Z_{k}}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{m\times m}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\otimes R_{W_{k}^{*}}\right)\left(\mathtt{A}(_{1^{*}}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}K(\cdot,W,\ldots,W)\xi)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})\mathsf{B}\right)

\displaystyle=~\mathtt{A}(_{1^{*}}\Delta_{R}^{{\ell}-1}K(\cdot,W,\ldots,W)\xi)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{{\ell}-1})(X^{\ell}_{k})^{*}\mathsf{B}

\displaystyle+~\mathtt{A}(_{1^{*}}\Delta_{R}^{{\ell}}K(\cdot,W,\ldots,W)\xi)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})[W_{k}^{*},\mathsf{B}].

Proof.

Since a similar proof as in the part (iii) in Proposition
4.4
together with the reproducing property of the generalized kernel elements given in Equation (
4.23
) yield the part (i) we only prove part (ii).

Let
f=(f_{1},\ldots,f_{r})\in\mathcal{H}(K)
and
\mathsf{S}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
so that
f\otimes\mathsf{S}\in\mathcal{H}_{m}
. Then, for
1\leq k\leq d
,
