Any nc holomorphic vector bundle with trivial cocycle is isomorphic to the trivial nc vector bundle.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the preceding theorem.
∎

5.2.3.
Noncommutative hermitian structures

Given a nc vector bundle, we now define the notion of a hermitian metric on each of its fibres what we call
nc hermitian structure
. Recall the notation:
\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}_{nc},{\mathbb{C}}_{nc})=\coprod_{n,m\geq 1}\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}^{n\times m})
.

Definition 5.18
.

A nc hermitian structure on a nc holomorphic vector bundle
E\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
of rank
r
over
\Omega
with
E\subset\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{V})
is an assignment

\mathsf{Her}:E\times E\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}_{nc},{\mathbb{C}}_{nc}),

satisfying following properties:

(1)

For every
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z\in\Omega_{n}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
, and
f\in E_{Z}
,
g\in E_{W}
,

\mathsf{Her}(f,g)\in\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}^{n\times m},{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times m}).

(2)

\mathsf{Her}(f,g)
is
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear in
f
for every
g\in E_{W}
and
W\in\Omega
.

(3)

For every uniformly open subset
\mathcal{U}\subset\Omega
and any two uniformly analytic nc sections
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow E
, the nc function

\mathsf{Her}(\sigma_{1}(\cdot),\sigma_{2}(\cdot)):\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}_{nc},{\mathbb{C}}_{nc})

of order
1
is a cp nc kernel.

A nc holomorphic vector bundle
E\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
equipped with a nc hermitian structure
\mathsf{Her}
is called a nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle.

Remark 5.19
.

For a trivial nc holomorphic vector bundle
\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H})\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
with some Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
, note that
\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H})\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
turns out to be a nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle equipped with the canonical hermitian structure obtained from the matrix sesquilinear form (cf.
[
21
, Chapter 3, Section 3.5]
): The given sesquilinear form
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
on
\mathcal{H}
induces a matrix sesquilinear form
\langle\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\rangle:\mathcal{H}^{n\times n}\times\mathcal{H}^{m\times m}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
for all
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
as follows:

( 5.4 )

(A,B)\mapsto\langle\!\langle A,B\rangle\!\rangle:=\left(\!\left(\langle a_{ij},b_{lk}\rangle\right)\!\right).

In consequence, a nc holomorphic vector bundle
E\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
embedded in
\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H})
inherits a canonical hermitian structure from the trivial bundle
\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H})\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
.

In general, for any uniformly open subset
\mathcal{U}\subset\Omega
and a local frame
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow E
, note that the nc hermitian structure on
E\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
gives rise to the cp nc kernel

K_{\mathsf{Her}}:\mathcal{U}\times\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}_{nc},({\mathbb{C}}^{r\times r})_{nc})

defined as

K_{\mathsf{Her}}(Z,W)(P)=\left(\!\left(\mathsf{Her}(\gamma_{i}(Z),\gamma_{j}(W))(P)\right)\!\right)_{i,j=1}^{r},

for
Z\in\mathcal{U}\cap{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n}
,
W\in\mathcal{U}\cap{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
,
P\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times m}
and
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Definition 5.20
.

Two nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundles
E\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
and
E^{\prime}\overset{\pi^{\prime}}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{\prime}
with hermitian structures
\mathsf{Her}
and
\mathsf{Her}^{\prime}
, respectively, are said to be isomorphic if there exists a nc vector bundle isomorphism
\widetilde{\psi}:E\rightarrow E^{\prime}
which also preserves the hermitian structures: For every
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z\in\Omega_{n}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
, and
f\in E_{Z}
,
g\in E_{W}
,
\widetilde{\psi}
satisfies

\mathsf{Her}^{\prime}\bigg(\widetilde{\psi}(f),\widetilde{\psi}(g)\bigg)=\mathsf{Her}(f,g).

Let
\widetilde{\psi}:E\rightarrow E^{\prime}
be a nc vector bundle isomorphism preserving the hermitian structures and
\mathcal{U}\subset\Omega
be a uniformly open subset over which
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}
is a local frame for
E
. Then
\psi(\mathcal{U})
is a uniformly open subset of
\Omega^{\prime}
, and assume that
\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\gamma^{\prime}_{r}
is a local frame for
E^{\prime}
. For
1\leq i\leq r
and
Z\in\mathcal{U}\cap{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n}
,

\widetilde{\psi}(\gamma_{i}(Z))=\Psi_{i1}(Z)\gamma^{\prime}_{1}(Z)+\cdots+\Psi_{ir}(Z)\gamma^{\prime}_{r}(Z)

implying that
\widetilde{\psi}
gives rise to a uniformly analytic nc mapping
\Psi:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{r\times r})_{nc}
. Since
\widetilde{\psi}
is a nc vector bundle isomorphism, it follows that
\Psi
is a
\coprod_{n=1}^{\infty}\text{GL}(nr,{\mathbb{C}})
valued uniformly analytic nc function on
\mathcal{U}
. Thus, under a local trivialization the condition for
\widetilde{\psi}
to preserve the hermitian structures of
E
and
E^{\prime}
turns out to be the existence of a uniformly analytic nc function
\Psi:\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\coprod_{n=1}^{\infty}\text{GL}(nr,{\mathbb{C}})
such that

K_{\mathsf{Her}^{\prime}}(\psi(Z),\psi(W))(P)=\Psi(Z)K_{\mathsf{Her}}(Z,W)(P)\Psi(W)^{*},

for
Z\in\mathcal{U}\cap{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n},W\in\mathcal{U}\cap{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
and
P\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times m}
, where
K_{\mathsf{Her}}
and
K_{\mathsf{Her}^{\prime}}
are the cp nc kernels obtained from the hermitian structures of
E\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
and
E^{\prime}\overset{\pi^{\prime}}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{\prime}
, respectively, with respect to the local frames
\{\gamma_{i}:1\leq i\leq r\}
and
\{\gamma^{\prime}_{i}:1\leq i\leq r\}
.

5.3.
Noncommutative Cowen-Douglas bundles

In this subsection, we first show that every tuple in the nc Cowen-Douglas class
\mathrm{B}(\Omega)_{nc}
gives rise to a nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle over
\Omega
. Then we prove that the unitary equivalence class of any nc Cowen-Douglas tuple is the same as the unitary equivalence class of the associated nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle. We begin with the following technical lemma.

Lemma 5.21
.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{r}
be a row Hilbert operator space obtained from a complex separable Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
and for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{M}_{n}
be the module
\mathcal{H}^{n\times n}
over
{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n}
with the module structure obtained by the left multiplication by matrices in
{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n}
. Assume that a subset
\{H_{1},\ldots,H_{k}\}
of
\mathcal{M}_{s}
is linearly independent over
{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
for some
k,s\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists
\delta>0
such that the set
\{A_{1},\ldots,A_{k}\}
with
A_{j}\in B_{nc}(H_{j},\delta)_{ms},1\leq j\leq k
, is linearly independent over
{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

First, we prove the desired result for
k=1
. Denote
H_{1}=H
and let
R^{j}
be the
j
-th row of the matrix
H
, for
1\leq j\leq s
. Note that the linearly independence of the set
\{H\}
of
\mathcal{M}_{s}
over
{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
is equivalent to the linearly independence (over
{\mathbb{C}}
) of the set of vectors
\{R^{j}:1\leq j\leq s\}
in
\mathcal{H}^{1\times s}\cong\mathcal{H}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathcal{H}
. In other words,
\{H\}
is linearly independent over
{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
if and only if the Gramian matrix
G_{0}
of the set of vectors
\{R^{j}:1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq s\}
in
\mathcal{H}^{1\times s}
is invertible.

Since
\{H\}
is linearly independent over
{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
,
G_{0}
is invertible and hence is a strictly positive matrix. Let
\lambda>0
be the minimum eigenvalue of
G_{0}
and observe that
\lambda
remains be the minimum eigenvalue of
G_{0}^{\oplus m}
, which is the Gramian matrix of the set of rows of the
m
-th amplification
H^{\oplus m}
of
H
. Now, recall from the definition of the row operator space norm that

\left\|{G_{0}^{\oplus m}}\right\|=\left\|{H^{\oplus m}}\right\|^{2}_{ms,\mathcal{H}_{r}},~~~m\in\mathbb{N}.

Consequently, for any
0<\delta<\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{2}
, the Gramian of the rows of any matrix
A\in B_{nc}(H,\delta)
turns out to be invertible verifying that the set
\{A\}
with
A\in B_{nc}(H,\delta)_{ms}
is linearly independent over
{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

For general
k>1
, a similar argument with
\mathcal{H}^{k\times 1}\cong\mathcal{H}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathcal{H}
(
k
times) instead of
\mathcal{H}
together with the observation that
(\mathcal{H}^{k\times 1})_{r}\cong\mathcal{H}_{r}^{k\times 1}
yield the desired result.
∎

Next, we use this lemma in the following proposition to prove the existence of local frame of the desired vector bundle associate to an element in the nc Cowen-Douglas class.

Proposition 5.22
.

Let
\boldsymbol{T}\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
and
Y\in\Omega_{s}
be a semi-simple point. Then there exist a uniformly open neighbourhood
\Omega_{0}\subseteq\Omega
containing
Y
and uniformly analytic functions
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}:\Omega_{0}\rightarrow(\mathcal{H}_{r})_{nc}
such that, for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in(\Omega_{0})_{ms}
,
\{\gamma_{1}(W),\ldots,\gamma_{r}(W)\}
is a basis for the free left submodule
\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}
of the module
\mathcal{H}^{ms\times ms}
over
{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
.

Proof.

First, we observe from Corollary
5.8
that
\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}
is a free left module over
{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n}
for
W\in\Omega_{n}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Fix a free left module basis
\{H_{1},\ldots,H_{r}\}
of
\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-Y}
over
{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
. We now use Theorem
3.8
, when
\mathcal{X}=\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
endowed with the natural operator space structure,
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{H}
with the row operator Hilbert space structure, the uniformly analytic nc functions
\mathcal{T}:\Omega\to\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})_{nc}
and
\mathcal{F}:\Omega\to\mathcal{H}_{nc}
, respectively, are given by

\mathcal{T}(W)=D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{F}(W)=0,\quad W\in\Omega.

Since
Y\in\Omega_{s}
is a semi-simple point, it follows from Theorem
3.8
that there exist
\rho>0
and uniformly analytic functions
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}:B_{nc}(Y,\rho)\rightarrow(\mathcal{H}_{r})_{nc}
such that

\gamma_{j}(Y)=H_{j},~1\leq j\leq r\quad\text{and}\quad\{\gamma_{1}(W),\ldots,\gamma_{r}(W)\}\subset\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W},

for
W\in B_{nc}(Y,\rho)_{ms}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Also, we have from Lemma
5.21
that there exists
\delta>0
such that the set
\{A_{1},\ldots,A_{r}:A_{j}\in B_{nc}(H_{j},\delta)_{ms},1\leq j\leq r\}
are linearly independent over
{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
. The theorem then follows for any uniformly open nc set
\Omega_{0}\subset\cap_{j=1}^{r}\gamma_{j}^{-1}(B_{nc}(H_{j},\delta))
.
∎

We now prove the existence of nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle associated to an element
\boldsymbol{T}\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
, for which the following technical result is needed.

Lemma 5.23
.

Let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
be a bi-full uniformly open nc set and
\{Y_{\alpha}\in\Omega_{m_{\alpha}}:\alpha\in\Lambda\}
be the set of all semi-simple points in
\Omega
,
\Lambda
be an indexing set and for every
\alpha\in\Lambda
,
B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})
be a nc open ball in
\Omega
. Then
\left\{\widetilde{B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})}:\alpha\in\Lambda\right\}
forms an nc open cover of
\Omega
where
\widetilde{B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})}
is the similarity envelop of
B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})
for each
\alpha\in\Lambda
.

Proof.

Let
W\in\Omega_{s}
be a reducible point. Then
W
is similar to
\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&B\\
0&C\end{smallmatrix}\right]
for some
A\in({\mathbb{C}}^{s_{1}\times s_{1}})^{d},C\in({\mathbb{C}}^{s_{2}\times s_{2}})^{d}
and
B\in({\mathbb{C}}^{s_{1}\times s_{2}})^{d}
. Assume without loss of generality that both
A
and
C
are irreducible so that
W^{\prime}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&0\\
0&C\end{smallmatrix}\right]
is a semi-simple point. Being bi-full
\Omega
is similarity invariant and therefore,
W^{\prime}=Y_{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in\Lambda
. Further, for
\rho>0
, note that
W
is similar to
W_{\rho}:=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}A&\rho B\\
0&C\end{smallmatrix}\right]
as
W_{\rho}=\mathsf{S}_{\rho}W\mathsf{S}_{\rho}^{-1}
with
\mathsf{S}_{\rho}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\rho&0\\
0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right]
. Thus, there exists
\epsilon>0
such that
W_{\epsilon}\in B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})
because
W_{\rho}\rightarrow W^{\prime}=Y_{\alpha}
as
\rho\rightarrow 0
. Consequently,
W\in\widetilde{B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})}
.
∎

Theorem 5.24
.

Let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
be a uniformly open bi-full nc domain,
\boldsymbol{T}\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
and
\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H}_{r})\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
be the trivial nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle. Then

( 5.5 )

E_{\boldsymbol{T}}:=\{(Z,F)\in\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H}_{r}):F\in\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-Z}\},

along with the canonical projection
\pi
restricted to
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}
defines a nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
of rank
r
over
\Omega
.

Proof.

First, observe from the definition of the nc Cowen-Douglas class
\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
that the canonical projection
\pi:E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\rightarrow\Omega
is surjective. Note that the fibre of
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
over
Z\in\Omega
is
\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-Z}
.

Let
W\in\Omega_{s}
be a semi-simple point for some
s\in\mathbb{N}
. It follows from Proposition
5.22
that there exists an uniformly open neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}_{W}\subset\Omega
around
W
and uniformly analytic functions
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}:\mathcal{U}_{W}\rightarrow(\mathcal{H}_{r})_{nc}
such that for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
Z\in\mathcal{U}_{W}\cap{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
,
\{\gamma_{1}(W),\ldots,\gamma_{r}(W)\}
forms a free left module basis for
\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-Z}
over
{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
. Consequently, for any
F\in\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-Z}
with
Z\in\mathcal{U}_{W}\cap{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
, there exist unique
\Xi_{1},\ldots,\Xi_{r}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
such that

F=\Xi_{1}\gamma_{1}(Z)+\cdots+\Xi_{r}\gamma_{r}(Z).

Now define
\varphi_{W}:\pi^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{W})\rightarrow\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{U}_{W}}({\mathbb{C}}^{r})
as

\varphi_{W}(Z,F):=(Z,\Xi_{1},\ldots,\Xi_{r}),

where
\mathfrak{T}_{\mathcal{U}_{W}}({\mathbb{C}}^{r})\overset{\pi_{1}}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{U}_{W}
is the trivial bundle of rank
r
. Note that
\pi_{1}\circ\varphi_{W}=\pi
and since both
\pi
and
\pi_{1}
are uniformly analytic nc functions, so is
\varphi_{W}
. For each
Z\in\mathcal{U}_{W}\cap{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
,
\varphi_{W}(Z,.):\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-Z}\rightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms})^{r}
is
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear as well as free left
{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
module isomorphism.

Since
\Omega
is bi-full, it follows from Lemma
5.23
that the family

\{\widetilde{\mathcal{U}_{W}}:W~\text{is semi-simple},~W\in\Omega\}

forms an open cover for
\Omega
. In consequence,
\{(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}_{W}},\widetilde{\phi_{W}})\}
constitutes a nc local trivialization for
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
. Here,
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}_{W}}
is the similarity envelop of
\mathcal{U}_{W}
and
\widetilde{\phi_{W}}
is the unique extension of
\phi_{W}
to
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}_{W}}
.

Further,
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
is equipped with the hermitian structure induced from the canonical one on the trivial bundle
\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H}_{r})\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
(cf. Remark
5.19
). It thus verifies that
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
is a nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle of rank
r
over
\Omega
.
∎

We conclude this section by proving that the nc vector bundles associated to the nc Cowen-Douglas tuples completely determine them up to unitary equivalence. Before continuing with this result we record the following basic property of nc Cowen-Douglas class.

Proposition 5.25
.

Let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
be a bi-full uniformly open nc domain and
Y\in\Omega_{s}
be a semi-simple point. Then
\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}\subset\mathrm{B}_{r}(B_{nc}(Y,\rho))_{nc}
, whenever
B_{nc}(Y,\rho)\subset\Omega
.

Proof.

From the definition of nc Cowen-Douglas class it is enough to show that

\mathcal{H}_{Y}:=\bigvee\{h_{ij}:(\!(h_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W},W\in B_{nc}(Y,\rho)\}=\mathcal{H}.

If possible suppose that
h\in\mathcal{H}
is perpendicular to
\mathcal{H}_{Y}
. Let
\{Y_{\alpha}\in\Omega_{m_{\alpha}}:\alpha\in\Lambda\}
be the set of all semi-simple points in
\Omega
,
\Lambda
be an indexing set and for every
\alpha\in\Lambda
,
B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})
be a nc open ball in
\Omega
on which there is a local nc frame
\{\gamma_{1}^{\alpha},\ldots,\gamma_{r}^{\alpha}\}
for
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
. Since
\Omega
is bi-full, it follows from Lemma
5.23
that
\{B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha}):\alpha\in\Lambda\}
forms an nc open cover of
\Omega
. Set

\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}:=\bigvee_{W\in B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})}\left\{\gamma_{t}(W)_{ij}:1\leq t\leq r\right\},~~~\alpha\in\Lambda

and
\Lambda^{\prime}:=\{\alpha\in\Lambda:h\perp\mathcal{H}_{\alpha}\}
. Then consider the uniformly open subsets

\Omega^{\prime}:=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\Lambda^{\prime}}B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})\quad\text{and}\quad\Omega^{\prime\prime}:=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}}B_{nc}(Y_{\alpha},\rho_{\alpha})

of
\Omega
. Since
\Omega
is connected,
\Omega^{\prime}\cap\Omega^{\prime\prime}\neq\emptyset
. Let
\hat{\alpha}\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda^{\prime}
be such that
B_{nc}(Y_{\hat{\alpha}},\rho_{\hat{\alpha}})\cap\Omega^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
. We now show that
h\perp\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\alpha}}
.

Since
h\perp\mathcal{H}_{Y}
, it follows that

\langle\!\langle h\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}},\gamma_{t}(W)\rangle\!\rangle=0,\quad 1\leq t\leq r,~~W\in B_{nc}(Y,\rho)_{ms},~~m\in\mathbb{N},

where
\langle\!\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\!\rangle
is the matrix sesquilinear form associated to
\mathcal{H}
(cf. Equation (
5.4
)). Then for each
1\leq t\leq r
, the uniformly nc analytic function
F_{t}:B_{nc}(Y_{\hat{\alpha}},\rho_{\hat{\alpha}})\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}_{nc}
defined by

W\mapsto\langle\!\langle\gamma_{t}^{\alpha}(W),h\otimes\mathrm{Id}\rangle\!\rangle

is identically zero on
B_{nc}(Y_{\hat{\alpha}},\rho_{\hat{\alpha}})\cap\Omega^{\prime}
. Consequently, by the identity theorem for nc analytic functions,
F_{t}
vanishes on
B_{nc}(Y_{\hat{\alpha}},\rho_{\hat{\alpha}})
,
1\leq t\leq r
. But this contradicts the choice of
\hat{\alpha}
unless
h=0
. Thus,
\mathcal{H}_{Y}=\mathcal{H}
.
∎

Theorem 5.26
.

Let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
be a uniformly open bi-full nc domain and
\boldsymbol{T},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
. Then
\boldsymbol{T}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}
are unitarily equivalent if and only if
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
is isomorphic to
E_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
as nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundles.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{T}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}\in\mathcal{B}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}})
be unitarily equivalent via a unitary operator
U:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}
. Then for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
U\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}
intertwines
D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}
and
D_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}-W}
. Furthermore, since
U
is unitary,

(U\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}})(\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W})=\ker D_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}-W},\quad W\in\Omega_{m},~~m\in\mathbb{N}.

Thus, we have a nc vector bundle isomorphism
\Phi:\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H})\rightarrow\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\widetilde{T})
defined by

(W,H)\mapsto(W,(U\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}})(H)),\quad W\in\Omega_{m},~~H\in\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H})|_{W},~~m\in\mathbb{N},

and
\Phi|_{E_{\boldsymbol{T}}}:E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\rightarrow E_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}}
is onto.

For
Z\in\Omega_{n}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
and
A\in\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H})|_{Z}
,
B\in\mathfrak{T}_{\Omega}(\mathcal{H})|_{W}
, note that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{Her}}\left(\Phi(Z,A),\Phi(W,B)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{Her}}\left((U\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n}})(A),(U\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n}})(B)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\!\left(\langle U(a_{ij}),U(b_{lk})\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}}\right)\!\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\!\left(\langle a_{ij},b_{lk}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\right)\!\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Her}(A,B)

verifying that
\Phi|_{E_{\boldsymbol{T}}}:E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\rightarrow E_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}}
is a nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle isomorphism.

Conversely, suppose that
\Phi:E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\rightarrow E_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}}
is a nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle isomorphism and
Y
be a semi-simple point in
\Omega_{s}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
. It follows from Proposition
5.22
that there exists a local nc frame
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}
for the nc bundle
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
over a nc ball
B_{nc}(Y,\rho)
at
Y
. Then
\Phi\circ\gamma_{1},\ldots,\Phi\circ\gamma_{r}
form a frame for
E_{\boldsymbol{\widetilde{T}}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
over
B_{nc}(Y,r)
. Also, for every
W\in\Omega
, note that

\Phi\left(\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}\right)=\ker D_{\boldsymbol{\widetilde{T}}-W}.

Set
\mathcal{H}_{Y}:=\bigvee\{h_{ij}:(\!(h_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W},W\in B_{nc}(Y,\rho)\}
and similarly
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Y}
. Observe that

\mathcal{H}_{Y}=\bigvee_{W\in B_{nc}(Y,r)}\left\{\gamma_{t}(W)_{ij}:1\leq t\leq r\right\}\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Y}=\bigvee_{W\in B_{nc}(Y,r)}\left\{\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W))_{ij}:1\leq t\leq r\right\}.

We now define a linear map
U:\mathcal{H}_{Y}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Y}
by

U\left(\gamma_{t}(W)_{ij}\right):=\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W))_{ij},\quad 1\leq t\leq r,~~W\in B_{nc}(Y,\rho).

Being a nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle isomorphism,
\Phi
preserves the hermitian structures on the fibres. In particular, for
Z\in\Omega_{n}
and
W\in\Omega_{m}
,
1\leq s,t\leq r
, we have that

\mathsf{Her}(\gamma_{s}(Z),\gamma_{t}(W))=\widetilde{\mathsf{Her}}(\Phi(\gamma_{s}(Z)),\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W))).

Consequently, it follows from the definition of the nc hermitian structure (see Definition
5.18
) that

\langle\gamma_{s}(Z)_{ij},\gamma_{t}(W)_{\ell k}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle\Phi(\gamma_{s}(Z))_{ij},\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W))_{\ell k}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}},

verifying that
U:\mathcal{H}_{Y}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{Y}
is unitary.

So it remains to show that
U
intertwines
\boldsymbol{T}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}
. Since for
W\in B_{nc}(Y,\rho)_{ms}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
D_{\boldsymbol{\widetilde{T}}-W}(\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W)))=0
,
1\leq t\leq r
, we have for each
1\leq i\leq d
and
1\leq j,k\leq ms
,

\left[\left(\widetilde{T}_{i}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}}\right)(\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W)))\right]_{jk}=\left[\left(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes R_{W_{i}}\right)(\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W)))\right]_{jk}=\left(\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W))W_{i}\right)_{jk}\\
=\sum_{l=1}^{ms}\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W))_{jl}W^{lk}_{i}=\sum_{l=1}^{ms}U(\gamma_{t}(W)_{jl})W^{lk}_{i}=U\left((\gamma_{t}(W)W_{i})_{jk}\right)=U\left(T_{i}(\gamma_{t}(W))_{jk}\right)

where
W_{i}=\big(\!\big(W^{jk}_{i}\big)\!\big)_{j,k=1}^{ms}
. As a result, for every
1\leq t\leq r
,
1\leq i\leq d
and
1\leq j,k\leq ms
, we have that

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{i}\big(U\big(\gamma_{t}(W)_{jk}\big)\big)=\widetilde{T}_{i}\big(\Phi\big(\gamma_{t}(W))_{jk}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\bigg[\bigg(\widetilde{T}_{i}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}}\bigg)\big(\Phi(\gamma_{t}(W))\big)\bigg]_{jk}=U\big(T_{i}(\gamma_{t}(W))_{jk}\big).

It thus verifies that
U
intertwines
\boldsymbol{T}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}
on
\mathcal{H}_{Y}
. Finally, since
\Omega
is bi-full, the result follows from Proposition
5.25
.
∎

Remark 5.27
.

In light of Theorem
5.26
, the unitary invariants of a tuple in the noncommutative Cowen-Douglas class correspond to those of the associated noncommutative hermitian holomorphic vector bundle. This naturally leads to the investigation of invariants of noncommutative hermitian holomorphic vector bundles, which we intend to explore in future work.

6.
Model for noncommutative Cowen-Douglas tuples

The objective of the first half of this section is to model a
d
-tuple of operators in the nc Cowen-Douglas class
\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
(
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
) over a nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space, while in the later half, we introduce the notion of “local operators” associated to a tuple in
\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
for each
W\in\Omega
.

6.1.
Construction of model spaces

Given an element
\boldsymbol{T}=(T_{1},\ldots,T_{d})
in
\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
with
T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
, we construct a nc functional Hilbert space consisting of uniformly analytic nc functions on some uniformly open subset of
\Omega
taking values in
\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc}
as demonstrated below. Here, we equip
\mathcal{H}
with the row operator Hilbert space structure and denote it as
\mathcal{H}_{r}
as before.

Since
\boldsymbol{T}\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
, it follows from Proposition
5.22
that there exists a uniformly open sudomain
\Omega_{0}\subset\Omega
and a uniformly analytic functions
\gamma_{j}:\Omega_{0}\rightarrow(\mathcal{H}_{r})_{nc}
,
j=1,\ldots,r
such that
\{\gamma_{1}(X),\ldots,\gamma_{r}(X)\}
is a basis for
\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-X}
viewing
\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}
as a left
{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
module for all
X\in(\Omega_{0})_{m}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
, where
D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}
is as defined in Equation (
5.1
).

First, we define the uniformly analytic nc mapping
\Gamma:\Omega_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}^{r},\mathcal{H})_{nc}
as follows: For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, consider the mapping
\Gamma_{m}:(\Omega_{0})_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1})^{r\times 1},\mathcal{H}^{\oplus m})
defined by the formula

\Gamma_{m}(X):({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1})^{r\times 1}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{\oplus m}\quad\text{as}\quad\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\xi_{1}\\
\vdots\\
\xi_{r}\end{smallmatrix}\right]\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{r}\gamma_{j}(X)\xi_{j},

\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{r}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1}
and define
\Gamma|_{(\Omega_{0})_{m}}:=\Gamma_{m}
.

Evidently,
\Gamma_{m}(X)
is a bounded linear map for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
X\in\Omega_{0}
, as it follows from the definition of the row operator Hilbert space structure on
\mathcal{H}
that

\left\|{\sum_{j=1}^{r}\gamma_{j}(X)\xi_{j}}\right\|=\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\gamma_{1}(X)&\cdots&\gamma_{r}(X)\end{smallmatrix}\right]\cdot\xi}\right\|\leq\left\|{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\gamma_{1}(X)&\cdots&\gamma_{r}(X)\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\right\|\cdot\left\|{\xi}\right\|,

where
\xi\in({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1})^{r\times 1}
is the vector
\begin{bmatrix}\xi_{1}&\cdots&\xi_{r}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}
.

Denote
\Omega^{*}_{0}:=\{W:W^{*}\in\Omega_{0}\}
and consider the Hilbert space
{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
endowed with the Hilbert-Schmidt inner product

\langle A,B\rangle_{HS}=\mathrm{trace}(AB^{*}),

denoted by
({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m},\mathrm{tr})
. For
m\in\mathbb{N}
, define
\Lambda_{m}:(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m},\mathrm{tr})^{\oplus r},\mathcal{H})
as follows:

\Lambda_{m}(W)((y_{1}v_{1},\ldots,y_{r}v_{r})):=v_{1}\gamma_{1}(W^{*})y_{1}+\cdots+v_{r}\gamma_{r}(W^{*})y_{r},

for all
y_{1},\ldots,y_{r}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1}
and
v_{1},\ldots,v_{r}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times m}
. Observe, for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in\Omega^{*}_{0}
, that
\Lambda_{m}(W)
is a bounded linear operator. Indeed, it follows from the definition of the row operator space structure on
\mathcal{H}
that

\left\|{v_{1}\gamma_{1}(W^{*})y_{1}+\cdots+v_{r}\gamma_{r}(W^{*})y_{r}}\right\|\leq M\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left\|{y_{j}v_{j}}\right\|_{HS}\leq rM\left\|{(y_{1}v_{1},\ldots,y_{r}v_{r})}\right\|_{({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m},\text{tr})^{\oplus r}},

where
M
is the maximum of
\left\|{\gamma_{1}(W^{*})}\right\|,\ldots,\left\|{\gamma_{r}(W^{*})}\right\|
, and we note, for any
y\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1}
,
v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times m}
, that
\left\|{yv}\right\|_{HS}=\left\|{y}\right\|\left\|{v}\right\|
. Finally, we define the bounded linear operator

U_{\Gamma}:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{N}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}(\Omega^{*}_{0},({\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc})\hskip 7.22743pt\text{as}\hskip 7.22743pth\mapsto U_{\Gamma}(h),

where
U_{\Gamma}(h):\Omega^{*}_{0}\rightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc}
is defined, for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{m}
, by

( 6.1 )

U_{\Gamma}(h)(W):=\Lambda_{m}(W)^{*}(h).

Here, we think of
f\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}(\Omega^{*}_{0},({\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc})
as a nc function on
\Omega^{*}_{0}
taking values in the nc space over
\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}}^{r})\cong{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
.

Note that
U_{\Gamma}
is an injective linear map. Indeed, if
U_{\Gamma}(h)=0
, then
h
is perpendicular to
v\gamma_{j}(W)y
for all
1\leq j\leq r
,
W\in(\Omega_{0})_{m}
,
v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times m}
,
y\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Consequently, it follows from part (ii) in Definition
5.1
that
h=0
. Let
\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
be the range of
U_{\Gamma}
. We now carry forward the hermitian product on
\mathcal{H}
via
U_{\Gamma}
to
\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
making
U_{\Gamma}
an unitary operator from
\mathcal{H}
onto
\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
.

Staring with a noncommutative
d
-tuple of bounded linear operators
\boldsymbol{T}=(T_{1},\ldots,T_{d})
in the nc Cowen-Douglas class
\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
, we thus have constructed a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
of uniformly nc analytic
({\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc}
valued functions on some subdomain
\Omega^{*}_{0}\subset\Omega^{*}
unitarily equivalent to
\mathcal{H}
via the unitary operator
U_{\Gamma}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
turns out to be a nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space and
U_{\Gamma}
intertwines the operators
T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}
on
\mathcal{H}
and the adjoint
M_{Z_{1}}^{*},\ldots,M_{Z_{d}}^{*}
of the left multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions on
\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
simultaneously as shown in the theorem below.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
be a uniformly open nc domain,
\mathcal{H}
be a complex separable Hilbert space and
T_{1},\ldots,T_{d}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H})
. Assume that the
d
-tuple of operators
\boldsymbol{T}=(T_{1},\ldots,T_{d})
is in
\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
. Then there exists a nc uniformly open subdomain
\Omega_{0}\subset\Omega
, a nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}(K)\subset\mathcal{N}\mathcal{C}\mathcal{O}(\Omega_{0}^{*},\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc})
with a cp nc kernel
K:\Omega_{0}^{*}\times\Omega_{0}^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}_{nc},{\mathbb{C}}^{r}_{nc})
and a unitary operator
U:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}(K)
such that

( 6.2 )

U\circ T_{j}=M_{Z_{j}}^{*}\circ U,~~~j=1,\ldots,d

where
\Omega_{0}^{*}=\{W:W^{*}\in\Omega_{0}\}
and
M_{Z_{1}},\ldots M_{Z_{d}}
are the left multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions on
\mathcal{H}(K)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{H}(K):=\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
and
U:=U_{\Gamma}
as above. Fix an arbitrary
1\leq j\leq d
and recall from Definition
5.1
that

(T_{j}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}})(\gamma_{i}(W))=\gamma_{i}(W)W_{j},

for all
1\leq i\leq r
,
W=(W_{1},\ldots,W_{d})\in(\Omega_{0}^{*})_{m}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Consequently, it follows for any
v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times m}
,
y\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1}
and
W\in(\Omega_{0}^{*})_{m}
, that

\displaystyle T_{j}\circ\Lambda_{m}(W)((y_{1}v_{1},\ldots,y_{r}v_{r}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{j}(v_{1}\gamma_{1}(W^{*})y_{1}+\cdots+v_{r}\gamma_{r}(W^{*})y_{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{1}\gamma_{1}(W^{*})W^{*}_{j}y_{1}+\cdots+v_{r}\gamma_{r}(W^{*})W^{*}_{j}y_{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m}(W)((W^{*}_{j}y_{1}v_{1},\ldots,W_{j}^{*}y_{r}v_{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m}(W)\circ L_{W^{*}_{j}}((y_{1}v_{1},\ldots,y_{r}v_{r})),

where
L_{W^{*}_{j}}
is the left multiplication operator on
({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m},\text{tr})^{\oplus r}
by
W^{*}_{j}
. So it verifies the following identity

T_{j}\circ\Lambda_{m}(W)=\Lambda_{m}(W)\circ L_{W^{*}_{j}},\quad W\in\Omega^{*}_{0}.

Now observe, for
h\in\mathcal{H}
and
W\in\Omega^{*}_{0}
, that

(U_{\Gamma}(T_{j}^{*}(h))(W)=\Lambda_{m}(W)^{*}(T_{j}^{*}(h))=(T_{j}\circ\Lambda_{m}(W))^{*}(h)=(\Lambda_{m}(W)\circ L_{W^{*}_{j}})^{*}(h)\\
=L_{W_{j}}\circ\Lambda_{m}(W)^{*}(h)=W_{j}U_{\Gamma}(h)=(M_{Z_{j}}(U_{\Gamma}(h))(W)

verifying the identity given in Equation (
6.2
). It remains to show that
\mathcal{H}(K)
possesses a nc reproducing kernel.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{m}
, consider the evaluation mapping
\mathsf{E}_{W}:\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}\rightarrow({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m})^{r}
defined by

\mathsf{E}_{W}(U_{\Gamma}(h)):=U_{\Gamma}(h)(W)=\Lambda_{m}(W)^{*}(h).

Since
\Lambda_{m}(W)
is bounded and
U_{\Gamma}
is unitary,
\mathsf{E}_{W}
is bounded for
W\in(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{m}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Consequently, the evaluation functionals
\mathsf{E}_{W,v,y}:\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
given by

( 6.3 )

\mathsf{E}_{W,v,y}(U_{\Gamma}(h)):=\langle U_{\Gamma}(h)(W)v^{*},y\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{m}}

is bounded for every
v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times m}
,
y\in({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{m\times 1}
,
W\in(\Omega^{*})_{m}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore, by Riesz representation theorem, for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{m}
and
v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m}
,
y\in({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{m}
, there exists unique vector
K_{W,v,y}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
such that

( 6.4 )

\langle U_{\Gamma}(h),K_{W,v,y}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}}=\mathsf{E}_{W,v,y}(U_{\Gamma}(h))=\langle U_{\Gamma}(h)(W)v^{*},y\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{m}}.

verifying that
\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
is a nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space of uniformly analytic
\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}},{\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc}
valued functions on
\Omega^{*}_{0}
(see
[
10
, Theorem 3.3]
). Moreover, the reproducing kernel associated to
\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}
turns out to be the cp nc reproducing kernel
K^{\Gamma}
(see Theorem
4.2
) defined as follows:

K^{\Gamma}:\Omega^{*}_{0}\times\Omega^{*}_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}_{nc},({\mathbb{C}}^{r\times r})_{nc})\quad\text{where, for}~n,m\in\mathbb{N},

K^{\Gamma}:(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{n}\times(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{m}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}^{n\times m},({\mathbb{C}}^{r\times r})^{n\times m})\quad\text{is the mapping}

( 6.5 )

K^{\Gamma}(Z,W)(P):=\Gamma(Z^{*})^{*}(P)\Gamma(W^{*}),\quad P\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times m}

We set
K=K^{\Gamma}
. Indeed, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{m}
,
h\in\mathcal{H}
,
v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times m}
and writing
y\in({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{m\times 1}
as
y=(y_{1}\cdots y_{r})^{\top}
with
y_{1},\ldots,y_{r}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times 1}
, we have that

\displaystyle\langle U_{\Gamma}(h),U_{\Gamma}(v\gamma_{1}(W^{*})y_{1}+\cdots+v\gamma_{r}(W^{*})y_{r})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle h,v\gamma_{1}(W^{*})y_{1}+\cdots+v\gamma_{r}(W^{*})y_{r}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle h,\Lambda_{m}(W)((y_{1}v,\ldots,y_{r}v))\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Lambda_{m}(W)^{*}(h),(y_{1}v,\ldots,y_{r}v)\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m})^{r}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle U_{\Gamma}(h)(W),(y_{1}v,\ldots,y_{r}v)\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m})^{r}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle U_{\Gamma}(h)(W)v^{*},y\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{m})^{r}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle U_{\Gamma}(h),K_{W,v,y}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}}

verifying that

U_{\Gamma}(v\gamma_{1}(W^{*})y_{1}+\cdots+v\gamma_{r}(W^{*})y_{r})=U_{\Gamma}(v\Gamma(W^{*})(y))=K_{W,v,y}.

Also for any
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{m}
,
Z\in(\Omega^{*}_{0})_{n}
,
u\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times n}
,
x\in({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{n\times 1}
and
v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times m}
,
y\in({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{m\times 1}
, note that

\displaystyle\langle K_{W,v,y},K_{Z,u,x}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle U_{\Gamma}(v\Gamma(W^{*})(y)),U_{\Gamma}(u\Gamma(Z^{*})(x))\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\Gamma}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle v\Gamma(W^{*})(y),u\Gamma(Z^{*})(x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle(u^{*}v)\Gamma(W^{*})(y),\Gamma(Z^{*})(x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}^{\oplus n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\Gamma(Z^{*})^{*}(u^{*}v)\Gamma(W^{*})(y),x\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{n})^{r}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle K^{\Gamma}(Z,W)(u^{*}v)y,x\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{n})^{r}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.2
.

In general, there is no canonical representation of the Hilbert space of nc uniformly analytic functions on which a given tuple
\boldsymbol{T}\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
is modeled as the tuple of adjoint of the left multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions on it. This is because there is no canonical choice of local frames
\{\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}\}
of the associated vector bundle
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\to}\Omega
. However, two such frames give rise to unitarily equivalent models spaces.

Let
\{\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}\}
and
\{\gamma^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\gamma^{\prime}_{r}\}
be two local frames over a same nc subdomain
\Omega_{0}\subset\Omega
. As in the discussion following Definition
5.20
, we then have there exists a uniformly analytic nc mapping
\Psi:\Omega_{0}\to\coprod_{n=1}^{\infty}
such that

\gamma^{\prime}_{i}(Z)=\Psi_{i1}\gamma_{1}(Z)+\cdots+\Psi_{ir}(Z)\gamma_{r}(Z),\quad Z\in\Omega_{0},~1\leq i\leq r,

where
\Psi=(\!(\Psi_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{r}
is an
r\times r
matrix of
{\mathbb{C}}_{nc}
valued uniformly analytic nc functions on
\Omega_{0}
so that, for any
Z\in(\Omega_{0})_{n}
,
\Psi(Z)\in\mathrm{GL}(nr,{\mathbb{C}})
. Consequently, the corresponding cp nc kernels — denoted by
K
and
K^{\prime}
— obtained from the construction above are related in the following manner: For
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z\in(\Omega_{0}^{*})_{n}
,
W\in(\Omega_{0}^{*})_{m}
and
P\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times m}
,

K^{\prime}(Z,W)(P)=\Psi(Z^{*})^{*}K(Z,W)(P)\Psi(W^{*}).

In other words, the associated Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}(K)
and
\mathcal{H}(K^{\prime})
are unitarily equivalent via the unitary

U:=M_{\Psi^{*}}:\mathcal{H}(K)\to\mathcal{H}(K^{\prime})\quad\text{defined by}\quad f(Z)\mapsto\Psi^{*}(Z)f(Z),

where
\Psi^{*}:\Omega_{0}^{*}\to\coprod_{n=1}^{\infty}
is the uniformly analytic nc function defined as

Z\mapsto\Psi^{*}(Z):=\Psi(Z^{*})^{*}.

Thus, the model spaces turn out to be unique up to unitary equivalence.

In certain special cases, there are canonical choices of the local frame. For instance, the case of the tuple
\boldsymbol{R}^{*}=(R_{1}^{*},\ldots,R_{d}^{*})
of adjoint of the right creation operators on
\ell^{2}(\mathcal{G}_{d})
as described in Example
5.3
.

6.2.
Local operators

The
local operators
associated to an element
\boldsymbol{T}=(T_{1},\ldots,T_{d})\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
are defined as follows.
First, for each
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in\Omega
, we construct a family of subspaces
\left\{\mathcal{N}_{\ell,W}\right\}_{\ell=1}^{\infty}
. If
W\in\Omega_{m}
define
\mathcal{N}_{1,W}
to be the
{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
bi-module generated by
\ker D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}
. Suppose that we have defined
\mathcal{N}_{\ell,W}
for
1\leq\ell\leq p
. Then
\mathcal{N}_{p+1,W}
is defined as
{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
bi-module generated by

( 6.6 )

(D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W})^{-1}\big(\mathcal{N}_{p,W}^{\oplus d}\big):=\big\{h\in\mathcal{H}^{m\times m}:D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}(h)\in\mathcal{N}_{p,W}^{\oplus d}\big\}

where
\mathcal{N}_{p,W}^{\oplus d}=\mathcal{N}_{p,W}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathcal{N}_{p,W}
(
d
times). Note that
\mathcal{N}_{\ell,W}
is an invariant subspace of
T_{j}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes R_{W_{j}}
,
1\leq j\leq d
, for each
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
as recorded in the following proposition.

Proposition 6.3
.

The subspaces
\mathcal{N}_{\ell,W}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
as defined in (
6.6
) are invariant under the action of the operators
T_{j}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes R_{W_{j}}
,
1\leq j\leq d
, for any
\boldsymbol{T}\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
,
W\in\Omega_{m}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

Recall from Theorem
6.1
that the operators
T_{j}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes R_{W_{j}}
are simultaneously unitarily equivalent to the operators
M^{*}_{Z_{j}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes R_{W^{*}_{j}}
,
1\leq j\leq d
, respectively, on a nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}(K)
with the nc reproducing kernel
K:\Omega_{0}^{*}\times\Omega_{0}^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}_{nc},{\mathbb{C}}^{r}_{nc})
where
\Omega_{0}\subset\Omega
is a nc subdomain and
\Omega_{0}^{*}=\{W:W^{*}\in\Omega_{0}\}
. Consequently, the proof follows from part (ii) of Corollary
4.11
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

A direct proof of this proposition without referring to the model space of the given tuple
\boldsymbol{T}
of operators can be obtained with the help of the following lemma.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
\boldsymbol{T}=(T_{1},\ldots,T_{d})\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
and
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}
be a local uniformly analytic nc frame of the nc hermitian holomorphic vector bundle
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega
associated to
\boldsymbol{T}
over a uniformly open subdomain
\Omega_{0}\subset\Omega
. Then, for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in(\Omega_{0})_{m}
,
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}
,
X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell}\in({\mathbb{C}}^{d})^{m\times m}
,
\xi\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
and
1\leq k\leq d
,
1\leq j\leq r
,

( 6.7 )

\displaystyle\big(T_{k}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{m\times m}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}\otimes R_{W_{k}}\big)\big(\mathsf{A}(_{1}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}\gamma_{j}(W,\ldots,W)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})\mathsf{B}\big)

\displaystyle=~\mathsf{A}(_{1}\Delta_{R}^{{\ell}-1}\gamma_{j}(W,\ldots,W)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell-1})X^{\ell}_{k}\mathsf{B}+\mathsf{A}(_{1}\Delta_{R}^{\ell}\gamma_{j}(W,\ldots,W)(X^{1},\ldots,X^{\ell})[W_{k},\mathsf{B}].

Proof.

Since
\boldsymbol{T}\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
and
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{r}
is a uniformly analytic nc frame for the associated nc vector bundle
E_{\boldsymbol{T}}\overset{\pi}{\rightarrow}\Omega_{0}
, it follows for
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in(\Omega_{0})_{m}
,
1\leq j\leq r
and
1\leq k\leq d
, that

T_{k}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}(\gamma_{j}(W))=\gamma_{j}(W)R_{k}.

Then the desired identity is obtained by differentiating the equation above repeatedly using the nc Leibnitz rule for nc different-differentials.
∎

We now define the
local operator

N^{(\ell)}_{\boldsymbol{T},W}
associated to
\boldsymbol{T}
at
W\in\Omega_{m}
of order
\ell
as

( 6.8 )

N^{(\ell)}_{\boldsymbol{T},W}:=D_{\boldsymbol{T}-W}\left|{}_{\mathcal{N}_{\ell+1,W}}\right.:\mathcal{N}_{\ell+1,W}\rightarrow\underbrace{\mathcal{N}_{\ell+1,W}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathcal{N}_{\ell+1,W}}_{d-\text{times}}.

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
\Omega\subset({\mathbb{C}}^{d})_{nc}
be a nc domain and
\boldsymbol{T},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}\in\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
. Assume that there is a unitary operator
U:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}
satisfying
(U\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}})(\mathcal{N}_{\ell,W})=\mathcal{N}_{\ell,W}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and

(U\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}})\left|{}_{\mathcal{N}_{\ell+1,W}}\right.N^{(\ell)}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}},W}=N^{(\ell)}_{\boldsymbol{T},W}(U\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}})\left|{}_{\mathcal{N}_{\ell+1,W}}\right.,

for some
W\in\Omega_{m}
. Then
U
intertwines
\boldsymbol{T}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}
.

Proof.

Since
U\otimes I_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}
intertwines
N_{\boldsymbol{T},W}^{(\ell)}
and
N_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}},W}^{(\ell)}
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, it follows that
U
intertwines
\boldsymbol{T}
and
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}}
on the closed subspace

\overline{\bigvee\{h_{ij}:(\!(h_{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{m}\in\mathcal{N}_{\boldsymbol{T},W}^{(\ell)},\ell\in\mathbb{N}\}},

which can be shown to be equal to
\mathcal{H}
as in the Corollary
4.11
.
∎

Remark 6.7
.

It is worth noting that the ideal hypothesis for Theorem
6.6
would be the existence of a family of unitary operators
U_{\ell}:\mathcal{N}_{\ell,W}\to\mathcal{N}_{\ell,W}
intertwining the local operators
N^{(\ell)}_{\boldsymbol{T},W}
and
N^{(\ell)}_{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{T}},W}
, for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. However, it is not clear, in that case, if a global unitary
U:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}
as given in the theorem above can be manufactured from the given family of unitary operators.

7.
A noncommutative Gleason Problem

In this section, we present a noncommutative analogue of the classical Gleason problem: For a bounded domain
D\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
and a Banach algebra
\mathcal{A}(D)
, the problem is to study the existence of functions
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}\in\mathcal{A}(D)
such that

f(z)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}(z_{j}-p_{j})f_{j}(z),~~~\text{for all}~~~z\in D,

whenever
f\in\mathcal{A}(D)
with
f(p)=0
for some
p=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})\in D
. However, Gleason problem can be studied from different points of view. For instance, given a reproducing kernel Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
of holomorphic functions on
D
so that the multiplication operators
M_{z_{1}},\ldots,M_{z_{n}}
by the coordinate functions on
\mathcal{H}
are bounded, one studies the existence of
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}\in\mathcal{H}
satisfying

f(z)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}(z_{j}-p_{j})f_{j}(z),

on some connected neighbourhood of
p
in
D
, for any function
f\in\mathcal{H}
with
f(p)=0
.

Paraphrasing we can restate this problem as the following identity

\ker(\mathrm{e}v_{p})=\mathrm{ran}\left(D^{*}_{M^{*}-\bar{p}\mathrm{Id}_{n\times n}}\right),

where
M^{*}-\bar{p}\mathrm{Id}_{n\times n}=(M_{z_{1}}^{*}-\bar{p}_{1}\mathrm{Id}_{n\times n},\ldots,M_{z_{n}}^{*}-\bar{p}_{n}\mathrm{Id}_{n\times n})
and
D^{*}_{M^{*}-\bar{p}\mathrm{Id}_{n\times n}}:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathcal{H}
is defined by
h\mapsto((M_{z_{1}}^{*}-\bar{p}_{1}\mathrm{Id}_{n\times n})h,\ldots,(M_{z_{n}}^{*}-\bar{p}_{n}\mathrm{Id}_{n\times n})h)
. In the following subsection, we introduce a noncommutative analogue of the Gleason problem for uniformly analytic nc functions and show that such a problem in the nc category is always solvable uniquely, unlike the classical case. We then make use of this in the later subsection to obtain a characterization of nc reproducing Hilbert spaces of uniformly analytic nc functions on a nc domain in
{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
so that the adjoint of the
d
-tuple of left multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions are in the nc Cowen-Douglas class.

7.1.
Noncommutative Gleason problem

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a commutative unital ring,
\mathcal{N}
be a module over
\mathcal{R}
and
\Omega
be a nc set in
\mathcal{R}^{d}_{nc}
. Consider a bimodule
\mathbb{M}
of nc functions
f:\Omega\to\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{R})_{nc}
over the free algebra
\mathcal{R}\langle z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}\rangle
and observe, for
s\in\mathbb{N}
, that the nc space
(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{R})^{s\times s})_{nc}
can be identified with
\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{R})_{nc}\otimes\mathcal{R}^{s\times s}
, and therefore, any nc function
F:\Omega\to(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{R})^{s\times s})_{nc}
can be viewed as

F=\sum_{j=1}^{n}f^{j}\otimes A^{j}

for some nc functions
f^{1},\ldots,f^{n}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{R})_{nc}
and
A^{1},\ldots,A^{n}\in\mathcal{R}^{s\times s}
. For
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in\Omega_{s}
, we define an evaluation mapping
\mathrm{ev}_{W}:\mathbb{M}\otimes\mathcal{R}^{s\times s}\to\mathrm{Hom}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{R})^{s\times s}
by

( 7.1 )

\mathrm{ev}_{W}(F):=\sum_{j=1}^{n}A^{j}f^{j}(W),

where
F=\sum_{j=1}^{n}f^{j}\otimes A^{j}
. Observe that the kernel
\mathbb{M}_{W}
of
\mathrm{ev}_{W}
turns out to be a right
\mathcal{R}\langle z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}\rangle
- left
\mathcal{R}^{s\times s}
submodule of
\mathbb{M}
. Then we ask the following question.

Given a nc set
\Omega\subset\mathcal{R}^{d}_{nc}
, a module
\mathcal{N}
over
\mathcal{R}
and a bimodule of nc functions on
\Omega
taking values in
\mathcal{N}_{nc}
, for a point
W\in\Omega_{s}
, if the right
\mathcal{R}\langle z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}\rangle
- left
\mathcal{R}^{s\times s}
submodule

\mathbb{M}_{W}:=\{F\in\mathbb{M}\otimes\mathcal{R}^{s\times s}:\mathrm{ev}_{W}(F)=0\}

coincides with the direct sum of the submodules

(L_{Z_{j}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{R}^{s\times s}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbb{M}}\otimes R_{W_{j}})(\mathbb{M}\otimes\mathcal{R}^{s\times s}),~j=1,\ldots,d,

where
L_{Z_{j}}
and
R_{W_{j}}
are the left and right multiplication by
Z_{j}
and
W_{j}
,
j=1,\ldots,d
, respectively.

For our purpose in this article, we study the nc Gleason problem in the case of the
{\mathbb{C}}\langle z_{1},\ldots,z_{d}\rangle
-bimodule
\mathbb{M}
of uniformly analytic nc functions on a nc domain
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
taking values in
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})_{nc}
, and prove that the nc Gleason problem is always solvable uniquely, unlike the classical case. However, the same proof can be adapted to solve the nc Gleason problem in full generality as introduced, provided the spaces of square matrices
\mathcal{R}^{n\times n}
over
\mathcal{R}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, are equipped with a “Hilbert-Schmidt type” pairing, that is, a function
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:\mathcal{R}^{n\times n}\times\mathcal{R}^{n\times n}\to\mathcal{R}
defined by

\langle A,B\rangle=\mathrm{trace}(AB^{\top})

with the property that
\langle A,X\rangle=0
for all
X\in\mathcal{R}^{n\times n}
if and only if
A=0
.

Let
\Omega
be a nc domain in
{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
, a normed linear space
\mathcal{V}
and
F:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
be a uniformly analytic nc function for some
s\in\mathbb{N}
. Since the nc space
(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
can be identified with
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})_{nc}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
, we can also view
F:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
as

F=\sum_{j=1}^{n}f^{j}\otimes\mathsf{A}^{j}

for some uniformly analytic nc functions
f^{1},\ldots,f^{n}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})_{nc}
and
\mathsf{A}^{1},\ldots,\mathsf{A}^{n}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
. Then we say
F

vanishes
at
W\in\Omega_{s}
if

\mathrm{ev}_{W}(F)=0.

On the other hand, viewing
F(W)
as the linear mapping
F(W):\mathcal{V}^{s\times s}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
given by

X\mapsto F(W)(X):=\sum_{j=1}^{n}f^{j}(W)X\mathsf{A}^{j},

we note that
\mathrm{ev}_{W}(F)=0
if and only if

\mathrm{trace}(F(W)(X))=0,\quad\text{for all}~~X\in\mathcal{V}^{s\times s}.

Indeed, it follows from the observation

\mathrm{trace}(F(W)(X))=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{trace}(f^{j}(W)X\mathsf{A}^{j})=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathrm{trace}(\mathsf{A}^{j}f^{j}(W)X).

Thus, solving the
nc Gleason problem
in the case when
\mathcal{R}={\mathbb{C}}
and
\mathcal{N}
is a normed linear space
\mathcal{V}
amounts to solving the following problem:

Given a nc domain
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
, a uniformly analytic nc function
F:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
for some
s\in\mathbb{N}
and a point
W\in\Omega_{s}
, assume that
F
vanishes at
W
as described above. Then the problem of interest is to ask if
F
can be factorized as

( 7.2 )

F(Z)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}(L_{Z_{i}}\otimes I_{s}-I_{ms}\otimes R_{W_{i}})F_{i}(Z),~~~Z\in\Omega_{ms},m\in\mathbb{N},

for some uniformly analytic nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
on
\Omega
taking values in the nc space over
(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})
. If such factorization holds, which conditions on
F
make
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
to be unique?

We first solve this problem algebraically without involving any topology on the spaces, and therefore, the functions in this set-up are only nc functions; no assumptions on the analyticity of corresponding functions are imposed here.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
be a right admissible nc set,
F:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
be a nc function and
W\in\Omega_{s}
for some
s\in\mathbb{N}
. If there exist nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
such that

F(Z)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}(L_{Z_{i}}\otimes I_{s}-I_{m}\otimes R_{W_{i}})F_{i}(Z),~~~Z\in\Omega_{m},m\in\mathbb{N},

then such nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
are unique.

Proof.

We begin by pointing out that
\Omega=\big(\coprod_{m=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{ms}\big)_{\text{d.s.e}}
, and therefore, by
[
36
, Proposition 9.2 (i) and Proposition A.3 ]
it is enough to find the required nc functions on
\coprod_{m=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{ms}
.

Assume that there are nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
on
\Omega
taking values in
(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
such that the equation (
7.2
) holds on
\coprod_{m\geq 1}\Omega_{ms}
. Then we show the uniqueness of the nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
by comparing their Taylor-Taylor coefficients with those of
F
from their respective Taylor-Taylor formula incorporated in Equation (
7.2
).

Let
\mathcal{G}_{d}
be the free monoid over
d
letters and
\beta\in\mathcal{G}_{d}
with
|\theta|=\ell
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Then we consider the Taylor-Taylor formula for
F
and
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
around the point
W\in\Omega_{s}
up to the order
\ell+2
. In other words, following Theorem
2.10
, there exist families of multilinear mappings
\{F_{\beta}:(({\mathbb{C}}^{d})^{s\times s})^{k}\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})^{s\times s}:\beta\in\mathcal{G}_{d},~|\beta|=k,k\leq\ell+1\}
,
\{F_{i,\alpha}:(({\mathbb{C}}^{d})^{s\times s})^{\ell}\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})^{s\times s}:\alpha\in\mathcal{G}_{d},~|\alpha|=k,k\leq\ell+1\}
,
i=1,\ldots,d
, each family satisfying the canonical intertwining conditions at
W
,
\text{CIC}(W)
(see Definition
2.4
), such that

( 7.3 )

F(Z)=\sum_{k=0}^{\ell+1}\sum_{|\beta|=k}\left(Z-I_{m}\otimes W\right)^{\odot_{s}\beta}F_{\beta}\\
+\sum_{|\beta|=\ell+2}\left(Z-I_{m}\otimes W\right)^{\odot_{s}\beta}\Delta^{\beta^{\top}}_{R}F(W,\ldots,W,Z),~~Z\in\Omega_{ms};

( 7.4 )

F_{i}(Z)=\sum_{k=0}^{\ell+1}\sum_{|\alpha|=k}\left(Z-I_{m}\otimes W\right)^{\odot_{s}\alpha}F_{i,\alpha}\\
+\sum_{|\alpha|=\ell+2}\left(Z-I_{m}\otimes W\right)^{\odot_{s}\alpha}\Delta^{\alpha^{\top}}_{R}F_{i}(W,\ldots,W,Z),~Z\in\Omega_{ms},~i=1,\ldots,d.

Also, writing
Z_{i}=Z_{i}-I_{m}\otimes W_{i}+I_{m}\otimes W_{i}
for each
i=1,\ldots,d
, we have that

( 7.5 )

Z_{i}\otimes I_{s}-I_{ms}\otimes W_{i}=Z_{i}\otimes I_{s}-I_{m}\otimes W_{i}\otimes I_{s}+I_{m}\otimes W_{i}\otimes I_{s}-I_{ms}\otimes W_{i},~~~i=1,\ldots,d.

Substituting
F(Z)
,
F_{i}(Z)
and
Z_{i}\otimes I_{s}-I_{ms}\otimes W_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,d
from Equations (
7.3
), (
7.4
) and (
7.5
), respectively in Equation (
7.2
), and then comparing the coefficients associated to the word
\beta
with
|\beta|=\ell
in the resulting equation we have that

\displaystyle(X_{1}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{g_{j}\beta}-(X_{1}\otimes I_{s})(X_{2}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{j,\beta}

( 7.6 )

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}I_{m}\otimes(W_{i}\otimes I_{s}-I_{s}\otimes W_{i})(X_{1}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{i,g_{j}\beta},

for all
j=1,\ldots,d
,
X_{1},\ldots,X_{\ell+1}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
. Here, each of the terms
(X_{1}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{g_{j}\beta},(X_{1}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{i,g_{j}\beta}
and
(X_{2}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{j,\beta}
belongs to the space
(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})^{ms\times ms}
, which is isomorphic to the space
((\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})^{s\times s})^{m\times m}
. Thus each of these elements, being an
m\times m
block matrix with entries in
(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})^{s\times s}
, is viewed as a linear operator from
(\mathcal{V}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}
into
({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}
. Also, writing
X_{1}=(\!(X_{1}^{ij})\!)_{i,j=1}^{m}
with
X_{1}^{ij}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
for
1\leq i,j\leq m
, we interpret the matrices
X_{1}\otimes I_{s}=(\!(X_{1}^{ij}\otimes I_{s})\!)_{i,j=1}^{m}
and
I_{m}\otimes(W_{i}\otimes I_{s}-I_{s}\otimes W_{i})
as elements of the space
({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{ms\times ms}
, which is isomorphic to
(({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{s\times s})^{m\times m}
. As such, these matrices are viewed as
m\times m
block matrices with entries in
({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{s\times s}
and act linearly on
({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}
. Consequently, it follows from the equation above that for any
X_{1},\ldots,X_{\ell+1}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
,
T\in(\mathcal{V}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}
and
1\leq j\leq d
,

\displaystyle\langle(X_{1}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{g_{j}\beta}(T),\varepsilon_{1}\otimes I_{s}\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}

( 7.7 )

\displaystyle=\langle(X_{2}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{j,\beta}(T),X_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon_{1}\otimes I_{s})\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}

where
({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}
is the Hilbert space obtained by taking
m
-th direct sum of
{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
equipped with the Hilbert-Schmidt inner product, and
(\varepsilon_{1}\otimes I_{s})
is the column vector
(I_{s},0,\ldots,0)^{\text{tr}}
in
({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}
. In particular for
m=1
, we have that

( 7.8 )

\displaystyle\langle F_{g_{j}\beta}(X_{1},\ldots,X_{\ell+1})(T),I_{s}\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle F_{j,\beta}(X_{2},\ldots,X_{\ell+1})(T),X_{1}^{*}\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}.

Note that the equation above with
m=1
defines the Taylor-Taylor coefficients of
F_{i}
for
i=1,\ldots,d
uniquely.
∎

Remark 7.2
.

We point out that Equation (
7.8
) can be used to prove the local existence of uniformly analytic nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
satisfying Equation (
7.2
) whenever
F
is given to be uniformly analytic.

For
\beta\in\mathcal{G}_{d}
and
1\leq j\leq d
, define the multilinear mappings
F_{j,\beta}:(({\mathbb{C}}^{d})^{s\times s})^{\ell}\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})^{s\times s}
as in Equation (
7.8
) and consider the formal Taylor-Taylor series
F_{j}(Z)
centered at
W
obtained from this family of multilinear mappings. Using the canonical intertwining conditions for
F
along with Equation (
7.8
), it can be seen that the family of multilinear mappings
\{F_{j,\beta}:\beta\in\mathcal{G}_{d}\}
satisfy Equation (
7.6
). Consequently,
F_{j}(Z)
,
1\leq j\leq d
, satisfy Equation (
7.2
) whenever they define uniformly analytic nc functions. Then another use of the canonical intertwining conditions for
F
at
W
and Equation (
7.8
) yields that the family of multilinear mappings
\{F_{j,\beta}:\beta\in\mathcal{G}_{d}\}
,
1\leq j\leq d
satisfy the canonical intertwining conditions. Finally, for
X_{2},\ldots,X_{l+1}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
,
X\in({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}
with
||X||_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}=1
and
T\in(\mathcal{V}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}
with
||T||_{(\mathcal{V}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}=1
, take
X_{1}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times ms}
to be the matrix whose first row is
X^{*}
and all other entries are the
s\times s
zero matrix. Then observe from (
7.7
) that

\displaystyle||(X_{2}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{j,\beta}||_{ms^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{||T||_{(\mathcal{V}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}=||X||_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}=1}\left|\left\langle(X_{2}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{j,\beta}(T),X\right\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{||T||_{(\mathcal{V}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}=||X||_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}=1}\left|\left\langle(X_{2}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{j,\beta}(T),X_{1}^{*}(\varepsilon_{1}\otimes I_{s})\right\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{||T||_{(\mathcal{V}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}=||X||_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}=1}\left|\left\langle(X_{1}\odot_{s}\cdots\odot_{s}X_{\ell+1})F_{g_{j}\beta}(T),\varepsilon_{1}\otimes I_{s}\right\rangle_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{s}\sup_{||X||_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}=1}||F_{g_{j}\beta}||_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell+1}_{\text{cb}}}||X_{1}||_{ms}||X_{2}||_{ms}\cdots||X_{\ell+1}||_{ms}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{s}\frac{1}{\delta^{\ell+1}}||X_{2}||_{ms}\cdots||X_{\ell+1}||_{ms}

where the first inequality is obtained by using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality on the Hilbert space
({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}
and the second one holds since
F
has Taylor-Taylor series expansion on
B_{nc}(W,\delta)
and due to the fact that

||X_{1}||_{ms}\leq\sqrt{\mathrm{trace}(X_{1}X_{1}^{*})}=||X||_{({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{m\times 1}}\leq 1.

Thus we have that

\limsup_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\sqrt[\ell]{\left\|{F_{j}}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}^{\ell}_{\text{cb}}}}\leq\limsup_{\ell\rightarrow\infty}\sqrt[\ell]{\sqrt{s}\frac{1}{\delta^{\ell+1}}}<\frac{\sqrt[2\ell]{s}}{\delta}<+\infty,~~~1\leq j\leq d

verifying that
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
are uniformly analytic functions on
B_{nc}(W,r)
with
0<r<\frac{\delta}{2\sqrt[2\ell]{s}}
.

However, the next theorem shows that
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
can be constructed explicitly from the given
F
and thus, they are actually defined globally on
\coprod_{m\geq 1}\Omega_{ms}
.

We now proceed to prove the existence of the nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
on
\Omega
, which take values in
(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
and solve the nc Gleason problem.

Theorem 7.3
.

Let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
be a right admissible nc set,
F:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
be a nc function and
W\in\Omega_{s}
for some
s\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
F
vanishes at
W
if and only if there exists nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
such that

F(Z)=\sum_{j=1}^{d}(L_{Z_{j}}\otimes I_{s}-I_{ms}\otimes R_{W_{j}})F_{j}(Z),~~~Z\in\Omega_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}.

Furthermore, the nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
can be determined from the difference-differential quotient of
F
by the formulae:

F_{j}(Z):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}\big(\varepsilon^{*}_{\ell}\otimes I_{(\mathcal{V}^{*})^{t}}\big)\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,Z)\big(\varepsilon_{k}\otimes I_{(\mathcal{V}^{*})^{t}}\big)\otimes\mathsf{A}^{i}E_{\ell k},~~~Z\in\Omega_{t},

where
E_{\ell k}
’s are
s\times s
elementary matrices,
F=\sum_{i=1}^{n}f^{i}\otimes\mathsf{A}^{i}
,
f^{i}:\Omega\to\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})_{nc}
, and
\mathsf{A}^{i}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
.

Proof.

We begin by pointing out that
\Omega=\big(\coprod_{m=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{ms}\big)_{\text{d.s.e}}
, and therefore, by
[
36
, Proposition 9.2 (i) and Proposition A.3 ]
, it is enough to find the required nc functions on
\coprod_{m=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{ms}
.

Since the existence of nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}:\coprod_{m=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{ms}\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
satisfying Equation (
7.2
) trivially verifies that
\mathrm{trace}F(W)(X)=0
for all
X\in\mathcal{V}^{s\times s}
, we only prove the forward direction.

Let
F=\sum_{i=1}^{n}f^{i}\otimes\mathsf{A}^{i}
for some nc functions
f^{1},\ldots,f^{n}:\Omega\rightarrow\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})_{nc}
and
\mathsf{A}^{1},\ldots,\mathsf{A}^{n}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
. Then for each
j=1,\ldots,d
and
Z\in\Omega_{t}
,
t\in\mathbb{N}
,

\Delta_{R,j}F(W,Z)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,Z)\otimes\mathsf{A}^{i}.

Also for each
i=1,\ldots,n
and
j=1,\ldots,d
, note that
\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,.)
can be thought of as a
s\times s
matrix of nc functions on
\{W\}\times\Omega
taking values in
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})_{nc}
as follows

\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,Z)=\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}E_{\ell k}\otimes f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z),Z\in\Omega_{t},

where we view
\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,Z)
as a linear mapping from
{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times t}
into
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times t}
which acts on a rank one matrix
u\cdot v
with
u\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times 1}
and
v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times t}
as

\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,Z)(u\cdot v)=\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}E_{\ell k}(u\cdot v)f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z),

and
\{\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{s}\}
is the standard ordered basis for
{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times 1}
. Here, we have used the notation

f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z):=\big(\varepsilon^{*}_{\ell}\otimes I_{(\mathcal{V}^{*})^{t}}\big)\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,Z)\big(\varepsilon_{k}\otimes I_{(\mathcal{V}^{*})^{t}}\big),\,\,\,Z\in\Omega_{t},

and
I_{(\mathcal{V}^{*})^{t}}
is the identity mapping on
(\mathcal{V}^{*})^{t}
.
For the rest of the proof, we thus write the nc function
\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,.)
as

\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,.)=\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}E_{\ell k}\otimes f^{i}_{\ell k},

and consequently,
\Delta_{R,j}F(W,Z)
, for
Z\in\Omega
, turns out to be

\Delta_{R,j}F(W,Z)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}E_{\ell k}\otimes f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z)\right]\otimes\mathsf{A}^{i}.

We now define the nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}:\coprod_{m=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{ms}\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
by the following formulae:

( 7.9 )

F_{j}(Z):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z)\otimes\mathsf{A}^{i}E_{\ell k},

and check that
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
indeed satisfy Equation (
7.2
).

It follows from Equation (
2.9
) that

( 7.10 )

(\mathsf{S}\otimes I_{s})F(Z)-F(W)(\mathsf{S}\otimes I_{s})=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\Delta_{R,j}F(W,Z)(\mathsf{S}Z_{j}-W_{j}\mathsf{S}),

for
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z\in\Omega_{ms}
and
\mathsf{S}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times ms}
. Here, we view
\mathsf{S}
as an
1\times m
row vector over
{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
and write

\mathsf{S}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathsf{S}_{1}&\cdots&\mathsf{S}_{m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]=\sum_{p=1}^{m}\epsilon^{*}_{p}\otimes\mathsf{S}_{p}
with
\mathsf{S}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{S}_{m}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
and the standard ordered basis
\{\epsilon_{1},\ldots,\epsilon_{m}\}
for
{\mathbb{C}}^{m}
.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
Z\in\Omega_{ms}
and
T\in\mathcal{V}^{ms\times s}
viewing as
m\times 1
column vector

T=\begin{bmatrix}T_{1}&\cdots&T_{m}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}=\sum_{q=1}^{m}\epsilon_{q}\otimes T_{q}

with
T_{1},\ldots,T_{m}\in\mathcal{V}^{s\times s}
, write
f^{i}(Z)=\big(\!\big(f^{i}(Z)_{pq}\big)\!\big)_{p,q=1}^{m}
,
i=1,\ldots,n
and observe that

\displaystyle\bigg\langle[(\mathsf{S}\otimes I_{s})F(Z)-F(W)(\mathsf{S}\otimes I_{s})](T),I_{s}\bigg\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\langle\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{S}_{1}&\cdots&\mathsf{S}_{m}\end{bmatrix}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\!(f^{i}(Z)_{pq})\!)_{p,q=1}^{m}\otimes A^{i}\bigg)(T),I_{s}\bigg\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\langle\begin{bmatrix}\sum_{p=1}^{m}S_{p}f^{i}(Z)_{p1}\otimes A^{i}&\cdots&\sum_{p=1}^{m}S_{p}f^{i}(Z)_{pm}\otimes A^{i}\end{bmatrix}T,I_{s}\big\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\langle\sum_{q^{\prime}=1}^{m}\varepsilon^{*}_{q^{\prime}}\otimes\bigg(\sum_{p=1}^{m}S_{p}f^{i}(Z)_{pq^{\prime}}\otimes A^{i}\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{q=1}^{m}\epsilon_{q}\otimes T_{q}\bigg),I_{s}\bigg\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg\langle\sum_{p,q=1}^{m}S_{p}f^{i}(Z)_{pq}(T_{q}A^{i}),I_{s}\bigg\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{n}\bigg\langle\sum_{i,q=1}^{m}f^{i}(Z)_{pq}(T_{q}A^{i}),\mathsf{S}_{p}^{*}\bigg\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}

\displaystyle=\bigg\langle\sum_{p=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{p}\otimes\bigg[\sum_{i,q=1}^{m}f^{i}(Z)_{pq}(T_{q}A^{i})\bigg],\sum_{p^{\prime}=1}^{m}\varepsilon_{p^{\prime}}\otimes\mathsf{S}_{p^{\prime}}^{*}\bigg\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times s}}

\displaystyle=\langle F(Z)(T),\mathsf{S}^{*}\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times s}}

where
\mathsf{S}^{*}=\sum_{p=1}^{m}\epsilon_{p}\otimes\mathsf{S}^{*}_{p}=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}\mathsf{S}^{*}_{1}&\cdots&\mathsf{S}^{*}_{m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\top}
. Here, the first equality holds due the following observation

\displaystyle\left\langle F(W)(\mathsf{S}\otimes I_{s})(T),I_{s}\right\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}=\sum_{q=1}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{trace}(f^{i}(W)(\mathsf{S}_{q}T_{q})A^{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{trace}(A^{i}f^{i}(W)(\mathsf{S}_{q}T_{q}))=0,

as
\mathrm{trace}(F(W)(X))=0
. We have thus shown that

( 7.11 )

\big\langle[(\mathsf{S}\otimes I_{s})F(Z)-F(W)(\mathsf{S}\otimes I_{s})](T),I_{s}\big\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}=\langle F(Z)(T),\mathsf{S}^{*}\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times s}}.

Now for each
1\leq j\leq d
,
Z\in\Omega_{ms}
and
T=\left[\begin{smallmatrix}T_{1}&\cdots&T_{m}\end{smallmatrix}\right]^{\top}\in\mathcal{V}^{ms\times s}
, note that

\displaystyle\Delta_{R,j}F(W,Z)(\mathsf{S}Z_{j})(T)

\displaystyle=\bigg[\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}E_{\ell k}\otimes f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z)\bigg]\otimes A^{i}\bigg)\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{S}_{1}&\cdots&\mathsf{S}_{m}\end{bmatrix}\big(\!\big(Z_{j}^{pq}\big)\!\big)_{p,q=1}^{m}\bigg]\begin{bmatrix}T_{1}\\
\vdots\\
T_{m}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}E_{\ell k}\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{S}_{1}&\cdots&\mathsf{S}_{m}\end{bmatrix}\big(\!\big(Z_{j}^{pq}\big)\!\big)_{p,q=1}^{m}\big(\!\big((f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z))_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}\big)\!\big)_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}=1}^{m}\bigg]\otimes A^{i}\bigg)\begin{bmatrix}T_{1}\\
\vdots\\
T_{m}\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}\sum_{q=1}^{m}\bigg(\sum_{p=1}^{m}E_{\ell k}\mathsf{S}_{p}\bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{m}Z_{j}^{pt}(f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z))_{tq}\bigg)(T_{q})A^{i}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigg[\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}\sum_{t=1}^{m}\bigg(\sum_{p=1}^{m}E_{\ell k}\mathsf{S}_{p}\bigg(\sum_{q=1}^{m}Z_{j}^{pq}\big(f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z)\big)_{qt}\bigg)(T_{t})A^{i}\bigg)\bigg]

and consequently, we have that

\displaystyle\big\langle\Delta_{R,j}F(W,Z)(\mathsf{S}Z_{j})(T),I_{s}\big\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}\sum_{t=1}^{m}\sum_{p=1}^{m}\sum_{q=1}^{m}\text{trace}\big(E_{\ell k}\mathsf{S}_{p}Z_{j}^{pq}(f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z))_{qt}(T_{t})A^{i}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}\sum_{q,t=1}^{m}\text{trace}\big(Z_{j}^{pq}(f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z))_{qt}(T_{t})A^{i}E_{\ell k}\mathsf{S}_{p}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{m}\bigg\langle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}\sum_{q,t=1}^{m}Z_{j}^{pq}(f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z))_{qt}(T_{t})A^{i}E_{\ell k},\mathsf{S}_{p}^{*}\bigg\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}

\displaystyle=\big\langle(Z_{j}\otimes I_{s})F_{j}(Z)(T),\mathsf{S}^{*}\big\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times s}}.

Here, the last equality holds since for each
1\leq j\leq d
,

\displaystyle\big(Z_{j}\otimes I_{s}\big)F_{j}(Z)(T)

\displaystyle=\big(\!\big(Z_{j}^{pq}\otimes I_{s}\big)\!\big)_{p,q=1}^{m}\bigg(\sum_{p^{\prime},q^{\prime}=1}^{m}E_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}\otimes\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}(f_{\ell k}^{i}(Z))_{p^{\prime}q^{\prime}}\otimes A^{i}E_{\ell k}\bigg]\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{t=1}^{m}\epsilon_{t}\otimes T_{t}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=1}^{m}\epsilon_{p}\otimes\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}\sum_{q,t=1}^{m}Z_{j}^{pq}(f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z))_{qt}(T_{t})A^{i}E_{\ell k}\bigg].

Thus, it has been shown, for each
1\leq j\leq d
, that

( 7.12 )

\big\langle\Delta_{R,j}F(W,Z)(\mathsf{S}Z_{j})(T),I_{s}\big\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}=\big\langle(Z_{j}\otimes I_{s})F_{j}(Z)(T),\mathsf{S}^{*}\big\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times s}}.

A similar computation also shows, for every
1\leq j\leq d
, that

( 7.13 )

\big\langle\Delta_{R,j}F(W,Z)(W_{j}\mathsf{S})(T),I_{s}\big\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}=\big\langle(I_{ms}\otimes W_{j})F_{j}(Z)(T),\mathsf{S}^{*}\big\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{ms\times s}}.

Finally, Equation (
7.10
), Equation (
7.11
), Equation (
7.12
) and Equation (
7.13
) together verify the validation of Equation (
7.2
) with the nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
as constructed above.
∎

Remark 7.4
.

When
\Omega
is uniformly open nc domain and
F
is uniformly analytic, the uniqueness of the functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
is followed from Equations (
7.12
) or (
7.13
), provided
\Omega
contains at least one invertible point
Z
or
W
is invertible, respectively. Indeed, any nc functions
G_{1},\ldots,G_{d}
satisfying Equation (
7.2
) must satisfy (
7.12
) and (
7.13
). Consequently, we have

(Z_{j}\otimes I_{s})F_{j}(Z)(T)=(Z_{j}\otimes I_{s})G_{j}(Z)(T)

and

\ (I_{ms}\otimes W_{j})F_{j}(Z)(T)=(I_{ms}\otimes W_{j})G_{j}(Z)(T),

for all
1\leq j\leq d
,
Z\in\Omega_{ms}
,
\mathsf{S}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times ms}
and
T\in\mathcal{V}^{ms\times s}
, and therefore, with the help of the added hypothesis it verifies the claim.

In the following theorem, we solve the nc Gleason problem when
\Omega
is a uniformly open nc set and
F
is uniformly analytic.

Theorem 7.5
.

Let
F:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
be a uniformly analytic nc function and
W\in\Omega_{s}
for some
s\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
F
vanishes at
W
if and only if there exists uniformly analytic nc functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}:\Omega\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
such that Equation (
7.2
) holds. Moreover, the functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
satisfying Equation (
7.2
) are unique.

Proof.

Since
\Omega=\big(\coprod_{m=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{ms}\big)_{\text{d.s.e}}
, by
[
36
, Proposition 9.2 (i) and Proposition A.3 ]
, it is enough to find the required nc functions on
\coprod_{m=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{ms}
.

In view of Theorem
7.1
and Theorem
7.3
, it is enough to show that the functions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
obtained in Theorem
7.3
are uniformly analytic nc functions whenever so is
F
. Recall from Equation (
7.9
) that
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}:\coprod_{m=1}^{\infty}\Omega_{ms}\rightarrow(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
are nc functions given by

F_{j}(Z):=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z)\otimes\mathsf{A}^{i}E_{\ell k},

where
F=\sum_{i=1}^{n}f^{i}\otimes\mathsf{A}^{i}
and for
1\leq\ell,k\leq s
, the functions
f^{i}_{\ell k}
are determined by the equation

\Delta_{R,j}f^{i}(W,Z)=\sum_{\ell,k=1}^{s}E_{\ell k}\otimes f^{i}_{\ell k}(Z),~~Z\in\Omega.

Since
F
is uniformly analytic nc function, so are the order
1
nc functions (cf.
[
36
, Theorem 7.44, pp 114]
)

\Delta_{R,j}F(\cdot,\cdot):\Omega\times\Omega\to\mathcal{L}\big({\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc},(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}\big),\quad 1\leq j\leq d.

In particular, for a fixed
W\in\Omega_{s}
, the functions
\Delta_{R,j}F(W,\cdot)
,
1\leq j\leq d
, turn out to uniformly analytic nc functions on
\coprod_{m\geq 1}\Omega_{ms}
taking values in
(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{V},{\mathbb{C}})^{s\times s})_{nc}
. Consequently,
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
are uniformly analytic nc functions completing the proof.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

Note that there is no speciality of the choice of uniformly-open topology in Theorem
7.5
. In fact, if
\Omega
is finitely open (respectively, open in disjoint open topology) and the nc function
F
is locally bounded on slices (respectively, locally bounded in disjoint open topology), the same line of argument as in Theorem
7.5
along with Theorem
7.3
and Theorem
7.1
yield locally bounded solutions
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}
to the Gleason problem in finitely open and disjoint open topologies, respectively. We refer
[
36
, Chapter 7, pp 83, 89]
to the readers for the definitions of finitely open and disjoint open topologies.

7.2.
Application to the noncommutative Cowen-Douglas theory

In this subsection, we obtain a sufficient condition on a cp nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space of uniformly analytic nc functions on a nc domain
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
so that the adjoint of the left multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions on this Hilbert space is in the nc Cowen-Douglas class over
\Omega
.

First, recall from Equation (
7.1
) that the evaluation mapping
\mathsf{ev}_{W}:\mathcal{H}_{s}\to({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m})^{r}
at
W\in\Omega_{s}
is, by definition,

\mathsf{ev}_{W}(F)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathsf{A}^{j}f^{j}(W),

where
F=\sum_{j=1}^{n}f^{j}\otimes\mathsf{A}^{j}\in\mathcal{H}_{s}
. Evidently,
\mathsf{ev}_{W}
is a bounded linear operator. We now compute the kernel of this operator in the following lemma.

Lemma 7.7
.

For
W\in\Omega
, the kernel of the evaluation mapping
\mathsf{ev}_{W}:\mathcal{H}_{s}\to({\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m})^{r}
at
W\in\Omega_{s}
is

\ker\mathsf{ev}_{W}=N^{\perp}_{W}=\{SK(\cdot,W)\sigma_{t}:S\in{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m},\,1\leq t\leq r\}^{\perp}.

Proof.

Let
W\in\Omega_{m}
for some
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
F\in\ker\mathsf{ev}_{W}
. Recall from the preceding subsection that
\mathsf{ev}_{W}(F)=0
if and only if
\mathrm{trace}F(W)(\mathsf{X})=0
for all
\mathsf{X}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}
. Thus, we have that

\big\langle F,\mathsf{X}K(\cdot,W)\sigma_{t}\big\rangle_{HS}=\mathrm{trace}((F(W)\sigma_{t})(\mathsf{X}))=\big\langle(F(W)\sigma_{t})(\mathsf{X}),\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}\big\rangle_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m\times m}}=0

verifying, in particular for
\mathsf{X}=E_{ij}
,
1\leq i,j\leq m
, that
\ker\mathsf{ev}_{W}\subset N_{W}^{\perp}
. The converse inclusion also follows from the similar computation as above.
∎

Theorem 7.8
.

Let
\Omega
be a nc domain in
{\mathbb{C}}^{d}_{nc}
and
\mathcal{H}(K)
be a nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space of uniformly analytic nc functions on
\Omega^{*}=\{W:W^{*}\in\Omega\}
taking values in
({\mathbb{C}}^{r})_{nc}
with the cp nc reproducing kernel
K:\Omega^{*}\times\Omega^{*}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}({\mathbb{C}}_{nc},({\mathbb{C}}^{r\times r})_{nc})
which is invariant under the action of the multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions from left. Assume that

(a)

For any
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in\Omega^{*}_{s}
, the generalized evaluation functional
\mathsf{ev}_{W}
is bounded from
\mathcal{H}_{s}
onto
({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{r}
;

(b)

for any
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{H}_{s}
satisfies the nc Gleason property, that is, every
F\in\mathcal{H}_{s}
with
\mathsf{ev}_{W}(F)=0
can be expressed as

( 7.14 )

F=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(M_{Z_{i}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\otimes R_{W_{i}}\right)F_{i},

for some
F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}\in\mathcal{H}_{s}
.

Then
\boldsymbol{M}^{*}=(M^{*}_{Z_{1}},\ldots,M^{*}_{Z_{d}})
lies in
\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{H}(K)
is invariant under the action of the multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions from left, it follows from Closed Graph theorem that they are bounded operators on
\mathcal{H}(K)
. For
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
W\in\Omega^{*}_{s}
, recall that
\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W}:\mathcal{H}_{s}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{s}^{\oplus d}
is the linear mapping

\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W}:=(M_{Z_{1}}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\otimes R_{W_{1}^{*}},\ldots,M_{Z_{d}}^{*}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\otimes R_{W_{d}^{*}}).

So the adjoint
(\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W})^{*}
of this operator turns out to be the map
(\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W})^{*}:\mathcal{H}_{s}^{\oplus d}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{s}
defined by

(F_{1},\ldots,F_{d})\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(M_{Z_{i}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}}-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}(K)}\otimes R_{W_{i}}\right)F_{i}.

Thus, the condition (b) in the hypothesis turns out to be

\ker\mathsf{ev}_{W}=\mathrm{range}(\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W})^{*}.

Since
\mathrm{range}(\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W})^{*}=(\ker\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W})^{\perp}
and
\mathsf{ev}_{W}
is onto
({\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s})^{r}
(from condition (a)), it follows that the dimension of
\ker\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W}
is
rs^{2}
. Also, the same identity implies that the range of
(\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W})^{*}
is closed and consequently, so is the range of
\boldsymbol{M}^{*}_{W}
. Finally, recall from Lemma
7.7
that
\ker\mathsf{ev}_{W}^{\perp}=\{\mathsf{S}K(\cdot,W)\sigma_{t}:\mathsf{S}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s},\,1\leq t\leq r\}
and observe that

\displaystyle\overline{\bigvee_{s\in\mathbb{N},W\in\Omega_{s}^{*},v,y\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s},1\leq t\leq r}\left\{v^{*}(\mathsf{S}K(\cdot,W)\sigma_{t})y:\mathsf{S}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s\times s}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\bigvee_{s\in\mathbb{N},W\in\Omega_{s}^{*},v\in{\mathbb{C}}^{s},\mathsf{y}\in({\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{s}}\left\{K_{W,v,\mathsf{y}}\right\}}=\mathcal{H}(K).

This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 7.9
.

From Theorem
7.3
, we observe that the condition in part (b) of the theorem holds for all nc reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces
\mathcal{H}(K)
which are closed under all partial right difference-differential operators: For any
f\in\mathcal{H}(K)
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in\Omega^{*}_{s}
and
1\leq j\leq d
,
\Delta_{R,j}f(W,\cdot)\in\mathcal{H}_{s}
. In general, we need this property only for a certain class of functions in
\mathcal{H}(K)
, namely, the family of all functions in
\mathcal{H}(K)
which are entries of all functions
F\in\mathcal{H}_{s}
with
\mathsf{ev}_{W}(F)=0
. However, note that this condition needs to be satisfied by all elements in
\mathcal{H}(K)
whenever
\mathcal{H}(K)
contains nc constant functions. In other words, the tuple of adjoints of the multiplication operators by the nc coordinate functions on a cp nc reproducing kernel Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}(K)
is in
\mathrm{B}_{r}(\Omega)_{nc}
if for every
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
W\in\Omega^{*}_{s}
,
1\leq i,j\leq s
, the nc functions
f_{W,\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}}:\Omega^{*}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{r}_{nc}
defined by

Z\mapsto f_{W,\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}}(Z):{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times n}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{1\times n},\quad u\mapsto\varepsilon_{j}^{*}\Delta_{R}f(W,Z)(\varepsilon_{i}u),

belong to
\mathcal{H}(K)
whenever
f\in\mathcal{H}(K)
. Also, observe that using Corollary
4.6
this condition can be reformulated as the following inequality: For
1\leq i,j\leq s
, there exist
c_{ij}>0
such that

c_{ij}K\big(Z,Z\big)-f_{W,\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}}(Z)\otimes f_{W,\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}}(Z)^{*}\succeq 0,\quad Z\in\Omega^{*}_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}.

In fact, one can choose a positive constant
c>0
such that this inequality turns out to be equivalent to the following:

cK\big(Z,Z\big)-f_{W,\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}}(Z)\otimes f_{W,\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}^{*}}(Z)^{*}\succeq 0,\quad Z\in\Omega^{*}_{n},~n\in\mathbb{N},~1\leq i,j\leq s.

The authors wish to thank Professor Joseph A. Ball for fruitful discussions on nc reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces.
